# Preface

This book presents artificial intelligence and machine learning framework for renewable energy systems. The machine learning models with respect to solar energy storage system predictions are analyzed in Chapter 1\. The fourth component of the Internet of things (IoT) system is the user interface; this helps the users to control IoT. Chapter 2 highlights the study and implementation of various types of fuzzy structures using rule-based interfaces for steady-state and transient analysis. Chapter 3 provides a survey of the role, impact, and challenges, and recommended solutions of IoT for smart buildings. Chapter 4 presents a comprehensive design of a low-cost smart single-phase energy meter monitoring system. Chapter 5 explains the IoT-based smart grid. Chapter 6 presents maximum power point tracking control using particle swarm optimization algorithm for photovoltaic (PV) panel affected by partial shading due to shadow casting. Partial shading casted on a PV panel will produce multiple peaks’ power characteristic curve, thus tracking the global peak becomes a challenge especially under dynamic changing partial shading condition. Chapter 7 discusses a wireless system for monitoring and controlling of an electrical substation using NodeMCU (Wi-Fi module), Arduino Uno (microcontroller), ThingSpeak server, and Blynk. The different parameters of the substation such as current, voltage, power factor, frequency, and temperature are monitored using various sensors and electronic components. Smart grid–based big data analytics using machine learning and artificial intelligence has been discussed in Chapter 8. Chapter 9 presents the IoT-based intelligent solar energy-harvesting technique with improved efficiency.

# 1 Artificial intelligence and Internet of things for renewable energy systems

Abinaya InbamaniDepartment of Electrical and Electronics Engineering, Sri Ramakrishna Engineering College, CoimbatorePrabha UmapathyDepartment of Electrical and Electronics Engineering, Sri Ramakrishna Engineering College, CoimbatoreKathirvel ChinnasamyDepartment of Electrical and Electronics Engineering, Sri Ramakrishna Engineering College, CoimbatoreVeerapandiyan VeerasamyAdvanced Lightning, Power and Energy Research (ALPER), Department of Electrical and Electronics Engineering, Faculty of Engineering, University Putra Malaysia (UPM), MalaysiaS. Venkatesh KumarDepartment of Electrical and Electronics Engineering, Sri Ramakrishna Engineering College, Coimbatore

#### Abstract

The sustainability incredibly insists in having innovations in renewable energy. To obtain an unsullied and a well-grounded environment, innovations in the present mechanisms have to be uplifted ensuring a predictive framework and an enormous outcome can be expected. The goal needs to be met to establish new research concepts and to move on the energy requirements in optimization of the existing machine learning (ML) framework. With the lot more renewable energy existing in nature, the two most variable and commonly used renewable energies are solar and wind. Its cons are nonuniformity of power and its dependency on external environmental factors. Due to this, sole dependence on renewable energy is not possible, and hence conventional power grid is also to be considered when any sort of predictive analysis needs to be done. Hence, more concentration is to be made on forecasting of renewable energy and on smart grids ensuring continuous equilibrium and balance within renewable energy and conventional grid. The electricity demand and supply of power can be predicted using ML algorithms ensuring better savings with operational costs. The two-way electricity and information flow will prove smart grid in future ensuring continuous monitoring of the network bringing more requirements for ML framework. The early warning systems incorporated by Germany symbolize a very good example of how ML algorithms analyze the real-time data from various renewable energy sources to analyze the total amount of energy requirement of the country. Google invention on DeepMind proves that the energy efficiency is 3.5 times more compared to the energy demands of the last 5 years. The innovations on intelligent home energy management systems prove promising energy usage with ML in real time. The consumer or the customer behavior can be predicted easily with intelligent techniques along with various other features like weather or climate modeling ensuring a complete and interoperable framework that suits the conventional grids. Artificial intelligence helps in sustainability of the grid with more focus on demand response. The energy management and operation cost management in smart grid is a promising feature incorporating ML algorithms in renewable energy. The optimization on managing the asset along with its maintenance ensures efficient management of power. The large amount of data collected brings data mining and prediction, thereby enabling more analytics on data. To establish such a kind of platform, various transfer models for solar and wind need to be applied along with irradiance to power models incorporating blending of information along with its categorization in ML algorithms. Feature selection methods along with the diversified algorithms in ML for diversified applications ensure continuous upgradation in framework rather than integrated approach.

## 1.1 Solar energy storage systems and grid-connected PV systems

To consider being an infinite feature, renewable energy is significantly acquired from natural resources. Since the natural resources are capable of providing renewable energy endlessly, it can be utilized infinitely. Regardless of any vulnerable pollutant’s emission, the renewable energy can be harnessed, which are considered as a significant feature. Renewable energy generation sources are very essential for providing green energy to remote rural areas as well as urban areas. Some of the renewable energy resources are solar power, wind power, biomass, ocean wave energy, geothermal energy, hydroelectric power, and tidal power. Renewable energy source is often termed as alternative energy resources, since it is considered being a replacement to fossil fuels and nuclear power. There are a few aspects of renewable energy as follows:

*   Existence of renewable energy is perpetual, and it is infinite in the environment.

*   Always able to be harnessed, unlimitedly.

*   Known being a clean alternative energy than fossil fuels.

*   Can be replenished consistently.

### 1.1.1 Overview of solar energy system

Solar energy is one amid massively available renewable energy source and best cleanest energy source. Solar energy’s key source is sun’s radiation obtained from the Sun. Solar-powered electrical generation depends on photovoltaic (PV) and heat engines. The solar energy usage is always anticipated to proliferation around the world in the forthcoming centuries. Mainly, middle and low latitudes might be the chief energy source which improves economy after global and local crises. Solar energy is pollution free and comparatively no greenhouse gases are emitted. Solar technologies are generally characterized by two methods: (i) passive solar and (ii) active solar. Both the technologies mainly rely on capture, convert and distribution of sunlight thereby enabling solar energy. These active solar techniques use solar panels and thermal collectors to harness the energy. Building orientation to the Sun is considered as a passive solar technique. Solar power is one amid best sunlight conversion processes into electricity, either in a straight line through PV or through concentrated solar power (CSP) method. A CSP method tracks the Sun for huge area focusing.

In solar energy supply, energy storage plays a pivotal role. A technology that preserves the energy as well as transforms it into electricity for supplying it according to the power demand of end-user is called energy storage system. Accordingly, if produced power exceeds the required level, the surplus energy can be preserved in order to be utilized at high demand. Based on the large size storage integration, the conventional power grid is designed and the idea of balancing between generation and demand, almost 40% of solar off-grid systems lifetime costs or even more might be attributed to the storage. The solar energy availability at any point on the Earth varies with year, with day and night, and with the weather conditions. So do electrical loads which might be covered by supply systems based on solar energy. Even though the utilization of energy storage is immensely inevitable in a power system, and direct electrical energy storage besides distributed small-scale storage is of wide range in off-grid applications, yet they are considered being insignificant in most of the electricity industries.

In grids, the increasing PV penetrations lead to high demand of energy storage and new opportunities with regard to distributed direct storage, since they are highly necessitated in the industry sector. In terms of new opportunities, optimal managing end user greatly desires patterns as well as aggregated network flows, enhancing end user reliability in addition to power quality, sharing system components balance amid PV, as well as storage equipment are predominantly considered. Distributed electrical energy generated from large-scale to small local production is guaranteed through solar–thermal concentration and PV plants. In power sector, there exist a few challenges of balancing the power generation and requirement with suffering from supply constraints and shortages in power, since the electricity demand is constantly increasing every day. The transformation from conventional sources to nonconventional sources is crucially necessitated with regard to balancing the ratio of power production and demand. The core objective of utilizing solar as energy is the augmentation of power production as well as energy dependability expansion based on social, ecological, independent, and financial beneficial properties. To balance the differing time patterns of loads and solar energy production, an energy storage must be included in almost all stand-alone energy supply systems. For resolving the electrification issues in areas where the grid is not available, solar PV-based stand-alone systems become a potential solution [[1](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_001), [2](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_002), [3](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_003), [4](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_004), [5](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_005)]. However, the following design challenges remain in this system, that is, (1) maximum power extraction from PV array; (2) preventing battery from overcharge and over discharge; (3) conversion from dc to ac; and (4) facility for sufficient voltage boosting. As a widespread renewable energy sources, PV solar system is highly utilized by the countries with tropical climate that are situated around equatorial area [[6](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_006), [7](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_007), [8](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_008), [9](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_009), [10](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_010), [11](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_011), [12](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_012), [13](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_013), [14](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_014)].

The movement of the Sun in a day, weather, and passing clouds greatly impacts over the production of solar energy and the solar PV’s output power [[15](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_015), [16](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_016)]. Though there are various benefits, like reduction of greenhouse emission, use of fossil fuel reduction in electricity and extemporizing energy independence quantity [[17](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_017)] is contributed by high penetration rate of PV generation systems, yet it may cause reverse power flow to grid and high network losses in grid generation because of high-generation power supplied at the time of low demand. In recent days, the significance of PV systems becomes lesser in electric infrastructure, besides their impact over entire quality and dependability of grid power turns out to be insignificant. However, for obtaining the emission reduction, enhanced infrastructure dependability, and energy independence, the state and federal endeavor for augmenting the PV systems penetration over local and regional utility grids.

But still PV generation possibly begins to adopt the significant negative impacts across overall grid, if PV penetration touches the maximum levels, that is, 5–20% of overall generation. #### 1.1.1.1 High-energy storage requirement in PV systems

In the perspective of technology and economy, the electricity utility companies face numerous difficulties due to the integration of high-penetration PV system, transmission, and distribution network. In output, both magnitude and change rate are considered being significant, the system might switch from full output to fundamentally zero.

The high-level PV penetration impacts are reduced to some extent due to PV’s distributed feature: on the grid, the intermittent generation effects over large regions will be as minimal. However, the worst-case possibilities need to be addressed by the utilities. For guaranteeing the balance of sufficient voltage and power quality, the area regulation is highly necessitated at the time of high transients. In case of low PV generation, convergence of energy demand is validated through some kind of backup generation. Besides, distributed PV generation quantity fetched online increases spinning reserve amount requirement, since most utilities necessitate “spinning reserve,” which is generally equal to power output produced [[18](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_018)]. The requirement for further spinning reserve/storage for intermittent PV generation backs up at a high penetration level. For accommodating a high PV penetration and further distributed besides the generation of electricity sources intermittently, such as enhanced flexibility, improving various load management, storage technologies integration, and even restricted curtailment for extreme events, the utility grid needs to be evolved entirely, in many ways. For defining the next-generation grid infrastructure that consists of aforementioned areas, there are lot of endeavors have been taken. On the basis of current grid and high-penetration PV, a contemporary work termed the energy storage as “ultimate solution” to enable the intermittent sources for addressing utility base load needs, besides regarded it as “a storage system that has the efficiency to store in 1 day and less” may perhaps enable PV for affording 50% order of system energy [[19](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_019)].

Instead of selecting sufficient sized system on the basis of commercially available technologies, there are many features involved in this solar incorporating energy storage accompanied by distributed PV generation in grid-connected uses. ### 1.1.2 Energy storage applications in PV systems

The owner of the integrated PV solar system can get the overall advantages from the perspectives of environment, finance, and operation altogether besides utility via peak shaving and reliability applications [[20](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_020)]. During the supply of generated energy at one time to a load, various applications like peak shaving, load shifting, in addition to demand response are involved. The electric grid turns out to be more reliable by outage protection besides power quality control apart from any regulatory along with rate-based actions [[21](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_021)]. The following segment confers a few applications.

#### 1.1.2.1 Peak shaving

This mainly helps in minimizing demand charges for a customer or diminishing loads in peak practiced through utility. Peak shaving in various PV systems greatly necessitates PV affording entire essential power beyond a definite threshold besides, if PV is inaccessible, inadequate energy storage for gap filling. Peak shave failure may lead to serious financial concerns in which customers’ rates mainly depend on peak demand in monthly basis. Consequently, reliability is a key element. For peak shaving implementation, controller in the system must be competent for power dispatching storage system whenever PV is unapproachable for satisfying load.

#### 1.1.2.2 Load shifting

Load shifting is same as peak shaving in a technical manner; however, its application is a great benefit to consumers. The concerns purchase the power on a time-of-use basis. Many peak loads happen late in day, after passing peak for PV generation. Storage in semination with PV might be done for reducing demand for utility power throughout the day with rate times higher via storage system.

#### 1.1.2.3 Demand response

When the electric utilities are concerned, the demand response is apt for load side management. Demand response permits the users from controlling the noncritical loads by turning it off. The critical loads can be turned on and shedding of the noncritical loads will pave a way for better load side management.

#### 1.1.2.4 Outage protection

PV storage system’s key advantage possesses capability for offering power to consumer when power is unobtainable (i.e., for the period of outages). The difference between residence and commercial should be done so that complying with utility safety regulations aimed for back-feeding power avoidance onto transmission and distribution lines during a blackout can be done.

#### 1.1.2.5 Power quality control

Power quality confirms voltage constancy, adjustment of phase angle, and extraneous harmonic content extraction from electric grid along with outage protection. Presently, UPS device does this function in customer side. When PV storage systems are considered, expansion for UPS can be done for better power storage.

#### 1.1.2.6 Microgrid

Highly sustainable and reliable microgrids are attained by renewable generation and energy storage. Once operating separately from local utility microgrids incorporated with the various PV storage systems might greatly exploit PV-generated electricity for power supply to various consumers. Energy storage is vital for ensuring microgrid-stable operation through load and supply variations management besides maintaining voltage along with frequency constant. Successful microgrid integration encompasses PV storage systems to greater utility grid infrastructure which encompasses countless welfares in operation to utilities as well as consumers, nonetheless, a high system control level is necessitated, and PV storage system powerful design.

### 1.1.4 Grid-connected PV systems

Solar PV technology is mainly utilized for electricity generation from solar energy. A grid-connected solar PV system is basically a solar energy panel system whose connection is done to the main power supply (“grid”). The system user utilizes electricity from both solar panel systems along with electrical grid which greatly enables user for drawing energy from grid during emergencies like on a cloudy day or throughout night. Solar PV systems classification is done into two systems such as grid-connected and stand-alone systems. Grid-connected PV systems mainly involve 99% of installed capacity in contradiction with stand-alone systems using batteries [[23](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_023)]. Grid-connected PV systems without battery are less expensive and necessitate lower care. The requirement of batteries is not essential in grid-connected PV. Power generated is uploaded to grid directly for transmission and distribution. In grid-connected PV systems, power conditioning unit mainly helps in DC power conversion created through PV array into AC power in accordance with utility grid voltage and power quality necessities.

### 1.1.5 Alternate energy storage systems and its applications

#### 1.1.5.1 Overview of energy storage

Renewable power (PV, solar–thermal, or wind) is considered to be fundamentally intermittent in addition to fluctuations. Suppose renewable power has turn out to be a chief base-load dispatchable power source, multi-MW capacity and multi-hour duration are crucial for electricity storage systems [[24](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_024)]. The advanced ESSs are mainly for storing electrical energy and generation is done through renewable energy sources. These sources varies depending on the climatic conditions. On assessment basis, battery structures required for electricity storage generated from renewable energy sources are of minimal cost, have large efficiency, have extensive cycle life, have mature technology, resist high ambient temperatures, have vast power and energy volumes, and are environmentally benign.

Though there are numerous commercially electrical ESS (EESS), single storage system does not meet entire necessities for a perfect EESS. There is an appropriate range for every EESS. Storage is considered to be a vital unit for storing unstable electric energy during wind and PV power generation, which is abruptly growing new renewable energy, along with supplying unstable energy to electric power system yet once more in essential moment. The energy storage unit’s nonexistence may lead to issues such as abrupt blackout due to unstable sunlight-dependent electricity supply [[25](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_025)]. This storage plays a vital role in electricity storage systems for households, medium-size system for industrialized commercial use, along with extra large system for power plants and substations such as frequency regulations. Normally, ESS operation categorization is specified below:

**The charging period:** This is appropriate through network electrical energy, throughout off-peak intervals when electrical energy exists at lesser expenses.

**The discharging period**: In peak times, stored energy in an ESS is greatly utilized. Also, in this period, network electrical energy possesses greater price besides distributed generation usage is further cheap to run. On ESS application basis, reduction or even eradicating renewable distributed generation uncertainties can be attained [[26](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_026)].

The most widely used approaches in ESSs mainly depend on DC type which is intertwined with power electronic devices for national power grid connection. Mostly, ESS variation can be delivered pertaining to technology, location, capacity, demand, and investment expenses. Indeed, energy storage is regarded as a dominant factor in renewable sources integration, possessing a substantial part in sustaining a robust and consistent new electricity system [[27](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_027)]. The power fluctuations can be greatly minimized enhancing electric system flexibility and facilitating electricity generated storage and transmitting through variable renewable energy sources like wind and solar [[28](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_028)]. Diverse storage technologies are deployed in electric power systems which might be chemical, electrochemical, mechanical, electrical, or thermal. Energy storage capacity mainly comprises storage medium, power conversion system besides plant balance.

#### 1.1.5.2 Technologies used in energy storage systems

ESSs offer an extensive technological methodologies array for managing power supply and creating a further resilient energy infrastructure besides carrying cost savings to utilities and customers. Various storage and storage technology types are appropriate for dissimilar applications, often estimated through the amount of time stored energy which is essential [[29](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_029)]. Storage is required for stabilizing and creating variable generation from solar and wind dispatchable (or “base load”), [30] EST classification is done in electrochemical storage as in batteries, electrical storage as in supercapacitors, magnetic storage, kinetic storage, and chemical storage. All these technologies pairing can be done with software that helps in controlling charge and energy discharge. Different technologies are used in ESS which are categorized as pumped storage, batteries, flywheel energy storage, superconducting magnetic energy storage (SMES), regenerative fuel cell (RFC) storage, compressed air energy storage (CAES), and so on.

##### 1.1.5.2.1 Pumped storage system

Gravity is considered to be a potent, unavoidable force contiguous at every time and one amid furthermost recognized ESTs’ pumped hydropower. Presently, it is regarded to be the greatest energy storage type which has pumped hydroelectric facilities in which utility-scale gravity storage technology is exploited for world betterment. Pumped hydroelectric facilities are considered to be a common energy storage process on grid which accounts for over 95% of storage in use currently. Throughout off-peak hours, water is pumped by turbines to an elevated reservoir through excess electricity. Whenever there is a high demand, reservoir opens allowing retained water flowing via turbines and produces electricity. This system is considered to be challenging task due to terrain requirement (an upper and water lower pool) and enormous footprint. Energy is stored in water form easing pumped hydroelectric storage in an upper reservoir, pumped from another reservoir at a lesser reservoir [[31](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_031)]. Throughout high electricity demand stages, power generation is done through releasing stored water via turbines in a similar way which is regarded as a traditional hydropower station. When there is lower demand, upper reservoir recharging is done through lower cost electricity from grid to pump water back to the upper reservoir. A reversible Pump-Turbine/Motor-Generator assembly performs as both pumps and turbines. Pumped storage stations are quite different from conventional hydroelectric stations in which they are net electricity consumer, because of losses sustained in pumping cycle from lower to upper reservoirs [[32](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_032)]. Nonetheless, these plants are usually greatly effective (round-trip efficiencies attaining >80%), and balancing load inside the entire power system is validated. Pumped storage facilities might be easy on the pocket for peak and off-peak price differentials reason and their probable for offering serious ancillary grid services.

##### 1.1.5.2.2 Batteries

A device encompasses several electrochemical cells for stored chemical energy conversion into electrical energy is termed as battery which has a positive terminal, or cathode, and a negative terminal, or anode. Electrolytes help in permitting ions for moving amid electrodes and terminals letting current to flow out of battery for task attainment. If advanced technology is utilized in batteries, materials have greatly improved reliability, output, and modern battery systems density, and scale economies have intensely minimized associated cost. Once advanced technology is used in batteries, materials having prominently improved reliability and output will be preferred. Battery ESSs (BESS) is appropriate for individual and hybrid applications, comprising ones in amalgamation with engines, turbines, and renewable [[33](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_033)], which offers huge power contained by very short response time. It aids energy provider’s resource everyday power along with critical services, such as grid balancing and peak shaving, in a consistent, economical manner by traditional EMS system which enhances power supply reliability, availability, and efficiency. BESS are greatly utilized for applications such as peak shaving, frequency control and load balancing, renewable integration via ramp rate control, and energy time shift which are different energy storage types in batteries. Few are lithium-ion (Li-ion) batteries, lead-acid batteries, redox flow batteries, vanadium redox flow batteries, nickel–cadmium batteries, sodium sulfur batteries, electrochemical capacitors, iron–chromium flow batteries, zinc–bromine flow batteries. ##### 1.1.5.2.3 Flywheel energy storage

A flywheel is nothing but a rotating mechanical device mainly utilized for storing rotational energy which might be called up instantly. A flywheel encompasses spinning mass in its center which is motor driven as well as spinning force initiates a device like a turbine is driven by spinning force for electricity generation slowing rotation rate. Flywheel recharging is done through motor for increasing its rotational speed another time. Flywheel technology comprises useful properties for enhancing current electric grid which has the proficiency of capturing energy from intermittent energy sources over time, as well as delivering uninterrupted power supply to grid. Flywheels have the ability for responding grid signals promptly, providing frequency regulation besides electricity eminence enhancements. Flywheel ESSs are one amid energy storage devices. They store energy mechanically in the flywheel rotor by rotating the rotor whereas chemical batteries store energy electrically. When we want to use the stored energy in the rotor, a generator is utilized for mechanical energy conversion to electrical energy. Flywheel systems are temperature insensitive; meanwhile, they operate in a vacuum containment. Therefore, the hybrid vehicle with flywheel systems might run deprived of any problem at very cold or hot areas. Also, flywheel systems might help in storing further energy per system weight in contradiction with chemical batteries. The flywheel system is the most efficient energy storage device deployed for various applications.

##### 1.1.5.2.4 Superconducting magnetic energy storage

SMES is an innovative technology for storing electricity from grid contained in coil magnetic field encompassed of superconducting wire with near zero energy loss. SMES is considered as a grid-enabling device for storing besides discharging large power quantities almost instantly. It has proficiency of releasing high power levels contained by a cycle fraction for abrupt loss or dip in line power replacement. Power quality plays a vital part in grid reliability maintenance particularly while dealing with increasing congested power lines demand as well high renewable energy sources penetration like wind in addition solar. SMES has cryogenically cooled superconducting coil along with power conditioning system ensuing in greater consistency when compared with present power storage devices. Whenever superconducting coil charging is done ideally, current decay might not occur and magnetic energy storing will be done indeterminately.

##### 1.1.5.2.5 Regenerative fuel cell storage

RFC operation might be accomplished as a fuel cell, consequently generating power, besides as an electrolyzer; hence, its own fuel can be generated. It is mainly regarded as an energy storage device. Power generation is mainly done by RFC systems as well as electrolytically their reactants regeneration is achieved by electrochemical cell stacks. ESSs with tremendously high specific energy (>400 Wh/kg) design is done for that utilizing lightweight pressure vessels for gases produced via reversible (unitized) RFCs. RFC acts as a key component for system power and energy component separation in contrast to battery systems. The system power output is obtained through fuel cell stack size along with system energy capacity relating hydrogen (and oxygen) storage subsystem size which are typically high-pressure gas tanks. It is greatly utilized in applications where comparatively massive electricity storage must be done, like energy storage for distant off-grid power sources, emergency or backup power generation, zero-emission vehicles, hybrid energy storage/propulsion systems for underwater vehicles, spacecraft, and high-altitude long-endurance solar rechargeable aircraft.

##### 1.1.5.2.6 Compressed air energy storage

CAES is a process of utilizing generated stored energy for future use. Energy generated throughout low-energy demand periods (off-peak) might be released for meeting greater demand (peak load) periods at utility scale. The air compression by the compressor is greatly attained by energy from solar or wind and even electricity from thermal power plant in the course of off-peak period. In addition, same air might be used for generating electricity throughout peak hour. CAES is accomplished in underground caverns as well as abandoned mines.

##### 1.1.5.2.7 Technological challenges for energy storage system

ESS is considered to be a potential challenge which is primarily driven by renewable and microgrid expansions in current power in infrastructure [[34](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_034)]. The main commercial applications of batteries are grid and electric vehicles. Large-scale storage is mainly accomplished across globally through these traditional storage systems such as Pumped Hydroelectric Storage (PHS) and Compressed Air Energy Storage (CAES) [[35](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_035)]. Few challenges of ESS are guaranteeing sustainability and accessing component materials, operation protection and flexibility, energy deliverability cost, international standards’ incentives for ESS investment and progress for ESS choice and implementation, weak grid and poor formed market, installation infrastructure, and maintenance necessities.

##### 1.1.5.2.8 Applications of energy storage systems

In the ESS framework, classification is accomplished for different applications like adaptable exchanging current transmission frameworks, limited scope, medium scale, enormous scope applications, framework adequacy, outflow control, top shaving, and surrendering office interests in peaking generators. Among which, few are used for medium-scale applications like pumped storage, compacted air energy stockpiling, flywheel, and superconducting attractive energy stockpiling (SMES) which has limit in MW ranges. The energy framework is the place where the components are kept in a coordinated manner to guarantee that it fulfills the purpose of generation, dispersion, control, and energy change [[36](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_036)]. The energy generated from the renewable sources is called renewable energy and it has various sources like sun, solar, wind, rain, tide, and geothermal. Hence, there are lot of alternatives of energy generation. Nowadays, there are a lot of advancements in energy systems, thereby enabling the monitoring of data remotely, hence, ensuring quality of service. ### 1.1.6 Machine learning framework along with its tools and types

#### 1.1.6.1 Basics of AI

Artificial intelligence (AI) is a group or a cluster of techniques which gives an attempt to mimic human intelligence, nature, and behavior. It was first coined by John McCarthy through a research project on AI at Dartmouth in the year 1956\. Hence, AI is a collection of theories, ideas, and techniques, which includes logics, probabilities, and computational techniques and imitates or resembles the human cognitive abilities. Applications of AI are numerous.

They are used in data mining, diagnosing of disease, recognizing speech, predictive analysis, smart email categories, plagiarism checkers, robot readers, fraud preventions, giving recommendations, smart personal assistants, playing chess, and so on. There are a lot of definitions of AI according to many scientists as represented in [Table 1.4](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_tab_004).

Table 1.4:Overview on AI according to the scientists view.

| Year | Scientists view | About AI |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1978 | Bellman | AI is when the activities associate with human thinking abilities like decision making, learning, and problem solving |
| 1981 | A. Barr and E.A. Feigenbaum | A system that exhibits human behavior like understanding concepts, understanding language, reasoning, learning, and solving problems |
| 1990 | Schalkoff | Reproducing intelligence in terms of computational process |
| 1990 | Kurzweil | Functions that require intelligence when it is operated by humans |
| 1991 | Rich and Knight | Study of making the computers do things |
| 1993 | Luger and Stubblefield | Automation and intelligent behavior |
| 1995 | Dean.et.al | A study of computer program that behaves intelligently |
| 1998 | Nilsson | Writing computer programs, solving mathematics, understanding languages, and driving an automobile requires intelligence which is an AI |

On the whole, AI is mimicking the behavior of humans in order to perform and entertain the human beings, instruct the people, reveal useful information, and do activities even better than human beings. To check whether the computer is thinking like a human or not, the Turing test is carried out. Alan Turing proposed a methodology where there are two opponents: human and machine that acts like a human. The type of conversation is a text-only channel and the evaluator will check for it. The evaluator if fails to reliably understand the difference between the machine and human, the machine has passed the test. In this way, the machines are analyzed whether they are following AI standard or not. [Figures 1.2](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_002) and [1.3](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_003) show the operation of the machine and AI machine.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_002.jpg)

Figure 1.2: Machine operation.

A machine will get the input and transform into the desired output depending on the circuits and sensors inbuilt in the machines. An AI machine will get the input and perform analysis on the data, come with the predicted output, and check whether the predicted output goes well with the actual output. The dataset is split into trained and test, and analysis is made from the data. If the new data enters into the dataset, it can be automatically added to the test data or train data with the help of codes. The AI has very levels depending on the way in which the AI competes with the human behavior. The narrow AI is where the machine performs tasks better than human beings. The general AI can be defined as the state where the accuracy level of human and the machine is the same. The strong AI is when the machines lead the human-level accuracy.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_003.jpg)

Figure 1.3: AI machine operation.

AI is a computer that tries to exhibit human-like properties. It tries to mimic the human behavior. The ML is a subset of AI which trains the machine so as to mimic human behavior. The training can be given by the humans or it can be trained by itself too. The programmers can give examples and train the machine and once the training is over, the machine starts to learn by itself which is possible. Deep learning (DL) is the subset of ML wherein the understanding about the source is made even more possible as shown in [Figure 1.4](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_004). Let us consider an example of image recognition; the DL has various layers in it to understand the image and to come out with the predicted outcome.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_004.jpg)

Figure 1.4: AI versus ML versus DL.

#### 1.1.6.2 Why machine learning?

The main reason to go for ML is because it has improved healthcare and agriculture field, eliminates the need to perform tedious task, reduces the accidents by decreasing the complications related to traffic, provides digital consultation, reduces the human error, and so on. The ML algorithms have been invented so long ago, and are gaining massive popularity nowadays because of the data. The huge amount of data received nowadays is massive that the ML tools give a very good prediction which was not the case in those days. The cost of storage and the cost of computing are declining nowadays, paving a way for ML to come in a big picture. More data can be stored and processed in recent times leading to more prediction. The data can be structured and semistructured or unstructured depending on the application.

The log files, tweets, blogs, audio, and videos are all good examples of semistructured or unstructured data. Lot of things are connected to Internet, wherein the survey studies tell that there are more Internet connected devices than the world population is considered. Nowadays, data scientists describe the data and use yotta bytes, and in future, brontobyte will be the measurement values. People are expecting the machine to behave like humans, thereby demanding cognitive computing. The devices and systems are becoming smarter and the expectations of the human beings are becoming huge day and day.

Wearable healthcare and fitness trackers, smart agriculture, connected vehicles, weather forecasting, smart cities, and tele medicine smart surveillance are all indeed a boon to the human beings. All the predictions and real-time analytics are possible with ML. The dataset is split into train data and test data, and the ML models are applied to it.

#### 1.1.6.3 Types of machine learning models

The machine needs to undergo three stages, which include data storage and abstraction, generalization, and evaluation of data. The data is stored and translated into the same in a broader way in data storage and abstraction stage. The knowledge interpretation of data takes place in generalization, and the feedback is provided to improve the data learnt in the later stage. There are three classifications of ML models as shown in [Figure 1.5](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_005).

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_005.jpg)

Figure 1.5: Types of machine learning models.

The supervised learning platform learns itself by the labeled data. This data can be used for accurate mapping between the input and output. The algorithms under supervised learning can identify the features exactly and hence prediction can be made accordingly. When two variables are considered, it can easily predict the relationship between them and an outcome is obtained. The facial recognition, signature recognition, predicting house prices, stock prices prediction, discovery of customers, and so on are good examples of supervised learning. It is of two types as shown in [Figure 1.6](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_006).

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_006.jpg)

Figure 1.6: Types of supervised learning.

The classification models under supervised learning are used when the output is a binary value. The answer when it is True or False, Pass or Fail, this model is used. The lazy learners in classification model will wait for the testing data to arrive. The eager learners will try to learn itself with the training data and will try to come out with the predictions. The most important classification algorithms are discussed. Logistic regression will depend on the one, two, or more independent variables and come to a classified output. The k-nearest neighbor algorithm will look into the nearest neighbor and try to label them. K is the value which is the number of neighbors that the algorithm checks. Naive Bayes classifier predicts the output depending on the probability of the features. Even if the features are dependent to each other, the probability is calculated separately. Stochastic gradient descent updates the training data when the derivative is calculated. Decision tree will have a root node and classification is done on the top-down method where the data is divided into small structures for accurate prediction of values. Larger decision trees are combined together to form a random forest. It is a metaseparator that ensembles all the decision trees in it. A margin and a hyperplane are created in the support vector machine (SVM), and when new points arise it looks into place where it needs to be located. Some algorithms along with the advantages, drawbacks and use cases are discussed in [Table 1.5](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_tab_005).

Table 1.5:Algorithms along with its advantages, drawbacks, and use case.

| Algorithms | Advantages and use cases | Drawbacks |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Logistic regression | How the group of independent variables will be affecting the inputs can be determined (Word classification, weather prediction) | It is suitable only when the output is binary |
| K-nearest neighbor | Robust to noisy data in training dataset (image or video recognition) | Computation cost is little higher |
| Naive Bayes classifier | Fast in nature when compared to others (spam filters) | Output depends on probability |
| Stochastic gradient descent | Ease of implementation is good (weight updation in neural network) | It is sensitive to scaling of features |
| Decision tree | Simplicity in visualizing the data (data exploration) | Unstable if complex tree arrives |
| Random forest | Accurate than decision tree (industrial applications) | Implementation is complex |
| Support vector machine | Effective for spaces of high dimension (business applications) | Probability estimation is difficult |

Once the trained and test data are subjected to the ML models, the following three values appear in the classification report: accuracy, F1 score, and precision and recall. Accuracy is the ratio of correctly predicted values to the total values which were observed. TP is the number of positive predictions. TN is the number of negative predictions. Recall the fraction of relevant case among the total instances. The average value of recall and precision value is known as F1 score. The curve drawn between TP and FP is called the ROC curve. Hence, accuracy of the model is known with the help of ROC curve. The unsupervised learning works on the unlabeled data. The reinforcement learning is a process where the machine is exposed to data and understands it on a trial-and-error basis. It learns itself from the past experience and tries to come out with the best solution for it. There are many algorithms under the above-listed models. The models may overlap sometimes, and hence a lot of training of data are required to come out with the best model for the problem statement. There are various steps required to choose the best algorithm required for the user application. The dataset needs to be understood by understanding the features, and the dataset is split into train and test data. The models are trained and evaluated, and the best classifier is chosen as shown in [Figure 1.7](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_007).

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_007.jpg)

Figure 1.7: Steps to execute machine learning model.

### 1.1.7 History and evolution of deep learning

DL, as a part of ML, utilizes methods to process information and impersonates the reasoning procedure; the abstractions can be achieved with the help of algorithms in DL. It makes use of the layers of algorithms which can be used to process information, and outwardly perceive objects. There are three important layers in DL named input layer which is the layer in the DL model, the output layer which is the last layer in DL model, and the hidden layer is in between input and output layers. Every layer has an activation function in it which helps in processing of data. The data is gone through each layer, utilizing the values from the previous layers. The scientists along with their concepts are as per [Table 1.6](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_tab_006).

Table 1.6:Scientists and their concepts.

| Year | Scientists | Concepts |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1943 | Walter Pitts | Threshold logic mimics the thought process |
| 1950 | Alan Turing | Machine learning prediction |
| 1952 | Arthur Samuel | The program of ML was created |
| 1957 | Frank Rosenblatt | DLNN |
| 1959 | Torsten Wiesel | Creation of simple and complex cells |
| 1960 | Henry J. Kelley | The back-propagation model was created |
| 1960 | Alexey Ivakhnenko and V.G. Lapa | Creation of deep learning networks |
| 1962 | Stuart Dreyfus | Chain rule was created |
| 1965 | Alexey Grigoryevich Ivakhnenko | Data handling methods were identified |
| 1979 | Kunihiko Fukushima | Hierarchical models design |
| 1986 | Rina Dechter | Introduction to DL models |
| 1989 | Yann LeCun | Back-propagation worked along with CNN |
| 1995 | Dana Cortes and Vladimir Vapnik | SVM techniques |
| 2001 | Gartner | Concept of big data |
| 2009 | Fei-Fei Li | ImageNEt |
| 2012 | Google Brain | Cat experiment |

Multilayer perceptron (MLP) layer has a feedforward network, with activation function rectified linear unit (ReLU). The activation function is linear in MLP. Supervised learning is preferred. Many layers of perceptron are used in MLP. The convolutional neural networks (CNNs) have activation function ReLU with cross-correlation. The pattern of data followed is hierarchical and the complex pattern is assembled into simple patterns of data. The biological process is inspired in CNN, and the convolution is performed at least in one of the layers. The CNN has four parts, namely, convolution, max pooling, flattening, and full connection. The various CNN models are shown in [Table 1.7](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_tab_007).

Table 1.7:CNN models.

| CNN | Authors | Convolutional layers, fully connected layers |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Alexnet | Krizhevsky et al. | 5, 3 |
| VGG | Simonyan et al. | 13–15, 3 |
| GoogLeNet | Szegedy et al. | 21, 3 |
| DenseNet | Loni et al. | 121, 1 |

The convolution is a procedure where maps are highlighted, and activation function is applied to the channel maps. The max pooling procedure empowers the CNN and helps to identify the picture. The flattening provides the information as an array so that the CNN can understand it. The full connection acts as a concealed layer and is shown in [Figure 1.8](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_008).

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_008.jpg)

Figure 1.8: Convolution.

Recurrent neural networks (RNNs) has directed graph acting as a connection between the nodes. The cyclic graph which is rolled is used with variable sequence of inputs as the internal memory. The types of RNN are shown in [Figure 1.9](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_009).

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_009.jpg)

Figure 1.9: RNN types.

Self-organizing maps have values of high dimension even though the views are of low dimension. The Boltzmann machines have good connectivity to solve problems and have models based on energy. This is used when the training is supervised. The interesting features in the datasets are analyzed in Boltzmann machines. The restricted Boltzmann machine is still in use which uses convolutional depth and the model is inherited in a complex manner, and the deep Boltzmann machine has multiple layers in which the hidden variables are present. The training procedure is both bottom up and up down. The spike and slab Restricted Boltzmann Machine have values in continuous manner with inputs to be real valued. The types of Boltzmann machines are shown in [Figure 1.10](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_010).

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_010.jpg)

Figure 1.10: Boltzmann machines types.

Autoencoders have feature learning in which an input, decoding along with the distance between them, needs to be known. The data coding is learnt in the unsupervised manner. It has various applications like dimensionality reduction, image processing, drug discovery, popularity prediction, machine translation, information retrieval, and anomaly detection. Comparison of models is shown in [Tables 1.8](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_tab_008) and [1.9](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_tab_009).

### 1.1.8 Machine learning in renewable energy

An ML model finds a lot of applications in energy systems. There are numerous parameters which need to be measured in solar panels, especially with respect to ESS in concern. The forecasting of solar power generation, prediction of energy demand, forecasting for daily solar radiation and wind speed, prediction of hourly global radiation, predicting power output of solar system, hourly global solar radiation prediction, forecasting in PV generation of power, prediction of average globe ration, analyzing the PV electrical characteristics, estimating the PV system module temperature, short-term forecasting of loads in microgrid are some of the few. The energy storage can either make use of the battery, heat, or pumped hydroelectric storage in general. The modeling and prediction are easier when the energy systems are integrated with the ML models. There are eight frequently used methodologies when the integration between the ML models along with the energy system takes place. They are artificial neural network (ANN), MLP, decision trees, DL, ensembles, wavelet neural network, and ANN-based fuzzy and advanced hybrid ML models [[40](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_040)]. The procedure to implement Machine Learning in renewable energy sector is shown in Figure 1.11.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_011.jpg)

Figure 1.11: Procedure to implement ML in renewable energy sector.

In the first stage, the problem is identified by analyzing the various renewable sources of energy. Stage 2 is to find which data needs to be monitored for efficient scheduling of loads. Stage 3 is to make a real-time analysis and application for a smartphone so as to make the portability easier. Stage 4 involves bringing solution for scheduling of loads by monitoring the sources if possible (solar panels in case of solar energy). Condition-based monitoring is cost-effective by looking into cost–benefit analysis [[41](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_041)] as shown in [Figure 1.12](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_012).

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_012.jpg)

Figure 1.12: Cost–benefit analysis.

#### 1.1.8.1 Solar parameter estimation

##### 1.1.8.1.1 Data acquisition from the solar panel

Similar cells are connected together to form modules. For a nominal voltage charging of 12 V, a maximum of 36 cells are needed. About 30 mA/cm² is the maximum current that can be delivered. If the panel is working under standard test conditions with no complications on damaged cell, shading, and others, then the expected outcome will help in prediction of loads and forecasting. The bypass diodes and blocking diodes should help in reducing the losses ensuring uniform production of units. The panel has been maintained properly ensuring no parts are mechanically corroded and are free from debris. The electrical checks have been carried out often assuring that the wiring is not damaged. The solar panels have to overcome optical losses, recombination losses, top contact design losses, grid spacing losses, and others to ensure the optimum output under Standard Test Conditions. The top contact coverage, antireflection coatings, and surface texturing can reduce the optical losses. The shading losses, resistive losses, and contact losses will indirectly affect the output units being generated. Hence, before the process of data acquisition, these criteria have been considered. In case solar energy is preferred, the panels need to be maintained properly for smooth and efficient operation of loads. The maintenance of panels will ensure that it is operated safely and efficiently. The solar panel maintenance is shown in [Figure 1.13](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_013).

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_013.jpg)

Figure 1.13: Solar panel maintenance.

Maintenance can be classified into predictive and corrective depending on the usage. The condition of the panels along with their frequency of arrival of data can be predicted very easily in preventive maintenance leading to prediction. The break down is analyzed in corrective maintenance. The monitoring system of the solar panels will give an overall idea on the performance of the panels. The information about the power levels can be collected and used for further analytics. The units generated and the energy consumption can be calculated easily. The data are acquired by the acquisition module fitted in the source which in turn send the value to the next stage of processing as shown in [Figure 1.14](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_014).

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_014.jpg)

Figure 1.14: Monitoring system of panels.

The number of units generated along with the energy consumption can be visualized using the mobile phones. Historical data can also be measured with the help of IoT and ML. The monitoring software gives an idea on the weather-based performance, thereby enabling the comparison of weather conditions along with the load conditions leading to more precise load forecasting and load scheduling. Hence, the overall picture of analyzing the data with respect to the source will vary. When solar panels are considered numerous parameters like system status, energy generation, temperature of panel, temperature of air, global irradiance, POA irradiance, wind speed, total AC power, and total DC power can be measured accurately with the help of sensors fitted in the solar panels. Big data are used to monitor the data at large scale. Dashboard reports will give an understanding of the solar plant performance. [Figure 1.15](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_015) gives an overall idea on the maintenance of the panel. The influencing factors of PV are analyzed, and entropies are considered for better prediction [[40](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_040)].

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_015.jpg)

Figure 1.15: Overall view on the maintenance of the panel.

##### 1.1.8.1.2 Literature survey on ML models with solar power generation and forecasting of loads

The output is completely dependent on the parameter selection of the PV modules. There are lot of parameters present in solar module which will affect the lifetime and efficiency if wrongly chosen. The maximum power, its density, fill factor, and open-circuit voltage have a lot of possibilities to decrease the cell performance. The open circuit is the maximum voltage generating condition at zero current. It depends on the generation of photo current which represents the open-circuit voltage. *k*[B] represents the Boltzmann constant, with *q* representing the charge of the electron, *J*[0] indicating the current density at saturation stage, and *M*[ph] talking about the photo current density. The conversion efficiency is another important parameter under consideration. The ratio of maximum power to the ratio of incident light determines the conversion efficiency. Hence, *V*[oc], *I*[sc], FF, *V*[mp], *I*[mp], and area under radiance are typical characteristics of the solar cell. With respect to the number of solar cells designed, the above-mentioned parameters are estimated and designed.

The dataset is obtained by considering all these values. The dataset is divided into validation data and test data, and evaluation is made by undergoing cross-fold validation technologies. When the values are predicted, the following factors have to be considered without neglecting. The blocking diode causes the loss of 0.6 V. If the light intensity reduces, it might again lead to losses. So, all these factors have to be considered for efficient dataset. Metaheuristics helps in estimating the parameters properly enhancing the prediction. The new set of parameters will be used for each iteration in the ML models. Lot of proposed methodology schemes work on improved moth optimization algorithm, firefly algorithm, particle swarm optimization (PSO), bat algorithms, and whale algorithms, as shown in [Table 1.10](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_tab_010).

Table 1.10:Various algorithms used for predicting the solar power generation.

| Authors | Algorithm used | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Lin et al. [[41](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_041)] | Improved moth optimization algorithm | Predict the solar power generation by estimating the parameters |
| Liu et al. [[42](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_042)] | Firefly algorithm | Optimization of the parameters and efficient predictions of parameters |
| Liu et al. [[43](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_043)] | Particle swarm optimization | Determine the parameters for solar forecasting models |
| Fan et al. [[44](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_044)] | Whale optimization, particle swarm optimization, and bat algorithm | Parameters estimation for predicting solar radiations |

Apart from the above listed algorithms, there are various other algorithms like artificial bee colony algorithms, genetic algorithms, ant lion optimization, Bayesian optimization, coral leaf optimization, harmony searches and dragonfly algorithms. With the help of these optimizations, the efficient dataset is obtained upon which the ML models are applied on to get the exact results. With the help of these outputs, the effective forecasting of solar power generation is made and forecasting can be done easily. Theta statistical methodology is used for knowing about the upcoming solar power generation. DL, ANN algorithms, auto encoder, MLP, nonlinear autoregressive exogenous (NARX) model, self-organizing map, back-propagation neural networks, and auto-regressive integrated moving average are to name a few. The paper [45] gives an analysis of various Machine learning models including auto Long Short-Term Memory, Gated recurrent units, Long Short-Term Memory and auto Gated recurrent units. Two techniques were deployed to enhance the accuracy which are called structural diversity and the data diversity. DL algorithm shows good performance results [[46](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_046)] when compared with other reference models. Twenty-one solar power plants’ energy outputs were taken and predicted. The energy demand needs to be predicted [[47](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_047)], and analysis was done on various ML models like SVM, regression trees, linear regression, and ensemble boosting. The MLPs show the better results when compared to other models.

Hybrid renewable systems are preferred wherein the forecasting model was used for prediction of daily radiation of solar energy systems and wind speed [[48](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_048)]. The RNN was found to predict accurately. Root mean square error (RMSE) values were taken into consideration to detect the precision where the NARX method has lowest value of RMSE when compared to other methodologies like MLP, autoregressive moving average, and persistence methods [[49](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_049)]. The 1-year data were measured and the PV system output was predicted with various ML algorithms and the results were compared with respect to the RMSE factor. Self-Organizing Feature Maps has the lowest RMSE and were found to give better results [[50](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_050)]. NARX and MLP has better results when the hourly data of solar radiation are fore casted [[51](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_051)]. Best ANN algorithms were decided based on various factors like absolute error, RMSE, and linear correlation coefficient. The ANN models were found to give good results predicting the solar radiation [[52](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_052)]. The PV electrical parameter and the accuracy of solar irradiance are measured, and the system has been developed with field support vector regression (soft sensor). The soft sensor which is introduced is implanted in various places, and results were validated [[52](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_052)]. The temperature of PV systems and the environmental variables were utilized for training parameters, and adaptive neurofuzzy inference technique was incorporated. RMSE and correlation coefficient are taken for comparison of values [[53](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_053)]. Solar radiation values were predicted by picking up the most relevant parameters and the forecasting was made. Nine parameters were taken into account, and adaptive neurofuzzy inference systems (ANFIS) selection procedure was carried out. The prediction performance was good for three inputs rather than two inputs [[54](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_054)].

The forecasting of PV electricity was done with a new methodology wherein the wavelet transform was utilized, and the structures and features were extracted using deep CNN [[52](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_052)]. Study showed that for better prediction, the switching-delayed PSO has better results. Initially, the database history was taken and split into function after which the entropy values were composed. The proposed model was tolerable and robust [[53](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_053)]. Prediction of loads by understanding of renewable energy sources was done. The system was developed incorporating Bayesian learning and the decomposition was by empirical mode [[55](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_055)]. [Table 1.10](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_tab_010) gives an overview of models used with more preference to solar radiations. Hence, for estimating the solar parameters, proper choice of algorithm and model prediction is necessary.

The AI, statistical method, and hybrid are various types of methodologies preferred for enhancing the output of the panel. The solar energy models when compared with various techniques have found to be exclusive for certain models as shown in Figure 1.16\. Hence, the data splitting, discretization, encoding features, normalization, standardization, feature selection, and mapping the missing values are done by various pre-processing methodologies. The temperature, irradiance, insolation, sunshine, humidity, clearness index, and fill factor are further considered for building the optimization and resolving the accuracy issues. The solar energy can be monitored using various technologies as mentioned in Figure 1.16.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-001_fig_016.jpg)

Figure 1.16: Monitoring system of panels.

The applications of ML on ESSs are numerous with special reference to lifetime estimation, state prediction, discovering, and diagnosing the faults. These identifications will improve the modeling of the machine giving better optimization. State of health (SOH) of a battery is usually found by the ratio of remaining charge in a cell to the ratio when charge is full. When the present condition of the battery is measured with respect to the ideal conditions, the state of charge (SOC) is enabled. The battery energy storage models are considered as the first case, and various approaches are discussed. The SOH of the batteries is found by understanding the Gaussian process regression (GPR) models. These models estimate the SOC along with SOH, Li-ion batteries capacity, Li-ion batteries SOC, online SOH, accuracy with less than 1% of error, charging position, idling position, and discharging position analysis.

Table 1.12:Machine learning model to under SOH and SOC.

| Richardson et al. [[64](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_064)] | GPR | Capacity of batteries |

Hence, the lifetime of batteries can be estimated and predicted easily as the SOH and SOC is known to us. [Table 1.12](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_tab_012) gives the analysis of the model with the result. The depth of discharge indicating the amount discharged from the fully charged battery is also considered. The shallow cycle and deep cycle batteries are meant to give usage of power supply till 25% and 50% of discharging, respectively. These processes are done before recharging. The remaining useful time in a battery gives better accuracy but it is difficult to predict it**.** Predicting the remaining useful time of a battery can be done by health indicator and GPR model application. When the charging process is carried out with constant voltage and constant current, the features are extracted from the health indicator with good modeling, and benefits are numerous as the accuracy is of high prediction. For fusion of algorithms with filters, a complete ensemble empirical mode decomposition methodology is a most promising technique present to enhance the predicted lifetime of the batteries. Identifying the defects is one method to ensure the consistency of the batteries used in solar panel. Specific energy, specific power, and specific capacity need to be considered. [Table 1.13](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_tab_013) shows various ML models present to determine the faults.

Fuel cells are another storage method in ESSs which will convert the chemical energy into electricity. Electrochemical process is followed. The optimized design of fuel cells can be done once the losses pertaining to it are understood. This will enact the prediction in a smooth manner. Polymer electrolyte membrane is a type of fuel cell that has a lot of applications in stationary and portable side. These cells have membrane electrode assemblies. A methodology regularizing the particle filter incorporating the least square SVM (LSSVM) is used to forecast the voltage change pattern [[77](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_077)]. LSTM model can also be used in predicting the remaining useful life of the fuel cell. Stacked sparse autoencoder is added with deep neural network (DNN) to predict the fault of fuel cells. The unlabeled data are fine-tuned and mapping is done [[78](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_078)]. LSSVM is used in most of the application of ESSs of fuel cells where the dataset is split into trained model and test model. The input vector and trained model are trained with respect to time in the initial stage, and compared with the test dataset. The sliding window enacts the output as well. To brief, the features are extracted and fine tuned, sparse autoencoder is stacked, the measurement matrix is done with online data and collected data from the system, and prediction is performed. These ML techniques help in proper designing of the fuel cell. A hybrid storage energy system is a combination of one or more storage systems. The time series curve is a combination of annual record with respect to generation, production, and weather condition. DNN combined with time series data is suitable for hybrid ESSs. These data help in making the dataset in a good fashion enabling unified architecture across the network. The interoperability standards have been ensured as the devices are numerous, and this heterogeneous network will cause a lot of security issues. Hence irrespective of the applications we use, storage system dataset is important. Hence, the agent system in reinforcement learning outperforms the work and enables uniform interface between the client and server. The agent behaves in the uncertain environment and works accordingly. The environment and the actions are learnt well by the agent by means of repeated learning and the agent receives awards if performed well. With the help of rewards further, process is moved forward. If there are no rewards, the process is again revised and studied again. This feedforwarding will make the agents learn the environment in a even more better manner. The speed and user friendliness of various ML models are considered, and the following analysis is made as shown in [Figures 1.17](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_017) and [1.18](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_fig_018) [[38](#9783110713794_001103.xhtml_b_9783110714043-001_ref_038)].

The complexity and the accuracy are analyzed for various ML models. The DL and ensemble techniques are complex, and the other methodologies like MLP, decision trees, ANFIS, and hybrids are reasonably complex when compared to SVM and other techniques.

### 1.1.9 Conclusions

The main issues in global warming are giving rise to renewable energy. Hence, prediction of renewable energy has become a big area of research to work on. When the environmental conditions are complex and the flexibility of loads is not uniform, there is requirement of machine learning model in the renewable energy sector to inculcate more prediction in the data. The data have to be analyzed carefully removing the noises, and proper processing of data ensures correct prediction. The ML models with respect to solar ESS predictions is analyzed in this chapter. When the ML models are analyzed with respect to speed and user friendliness, the following analysis is made. The MLP, ensemble, ANFIS, and hybrid speed are reasonably high when compared with SVM and other techniques. When the relative study analysis of ML models in energy systems with respect to user friendliness is considered, the hybrid model stands relatively high and ELM model stands reasonably high. ANN, SVM, DT, Ensemble and WNN has low user friendliness and the models like MLP, DL and ANFIS has reasonable friendliness for the ML models in energy systems. The solar radiation can be forecasted with various ML models, and the performance can be predicted. Hence, ML has solved a range of issues in the ESSs by understanding their state, diagnosing the fault and predicting their lifetime. Algorithms combine multiply to provide a unified approach to the dataset. Linear regression algorithms are suitable for dataset, the nonlinearity in dataset may pose a trouble with its complex featurisms, and SVR has lot of advantages on its nonlinearity and has better accuracy. In future, algorithms and models in ML can be further optimized to meet the requirements of ESS. Data preprocessing, data munging, and data translation can be carried out efficiently to predict the exact behavior of modules. Failure detection in nanoscale and macroscale fabrication also needs to be considered in future research work. More research can be focused on smart ESS, where IoT can be combined with the ML [89] for obtaining the dataset. The quality of service in application-layer protocols of IoT will enhance the user friendliness and improve the quality outcome. The heterogeneous networks can be connected together with standardization, and future implications can be more on the industrial IoT too. The security is the major concern when such an integration is enabled and hence proper firewalls and VPN can help in protecting the data. Authorization, access control, and authentication methodologies can be an additional protection to the data. Hence, future trends are more on smart energy systems incorporating IoT with ML.
