# 4 Design and implementation of IoT-enabled smart single-phase energy meter monitoring system

Zhang RuiChooFaculty of Engineering, Technology and Built Environment, UCSI University, Kuala LumpurHungyang LeongFaculty of Engineering, Technology and Built Environment, UCSI University, Kuala LumpurRodney H. G. TanFaculty of Engineering, Technology and Built Environment, UCSI University, Kuala Lumpur

#### Abstract

Optimal energy usage in smart buildings, smart office, as well as smart homes brings significant impact to the economic growth and sustainability of a country. With the internet of things (IoT) technology, energy management can be performed more effectively through real-time monitoring and control. This book chapter presents a comprehensive design of a low-cost smart single-phase energy meter monitoring system. The architecture of the single-phase energy meter monitoring system comprises the data-capturing device, central processing unit and an IoT platform. The book chapter will be organized in several subtopics. It covers the hardware and software implementation which include the setup, configuration and system integration of the Modbus remote terminal unit (RTU) communication protocol, microcontroller, Wi-Fi integration and IoT platform. A detailed explanation on the configuration of the Modbus communication protocol according to IEEE-754 single-precision floating-point standard for meter readings will be presented. This information is then sent and stored to IoT platform channel via Wi-Fi connection on an every-minute basis via the user dashboard to show the trends of the electricity information over the time in detail. The operation process flow on the single-phase IEC standard energy meter monitoring system will be described. This book chapter will explain the considerations to be taken in the hardware selection, prototype design and construction to achieve reliability and safety. The results show that an online dashboard via IoT platform was successfully implemented to allow user to monitor their electricity information remotely on a real-time basis. The energy meter prototype is able to read the typical energy such as voltage, current, frequency, active power and power factor from a load every minute. The measurement is successfully uploaded to the cloud database and monitored via the IoT platform. Besides that, the energy information as measured by the prototype showing similar readings is compared to industry-grade, Janitza digital power meter. As a conclusion, the low-cost prototype energy meter is suitable to be used for electricity consumer from residential as well as small-scale business owners to have better understanding on their energy consumption patterns in a day, weeks and months. The system design can be adopted with any device using Modbus RTU protocol to build an IoT smart monitoring system. By using Modbus RTU, subsequently, it can be used for various load profiling analysis to manage and control electricity cost in future.

Keywords: energy monitoring, smart meter, internet of things,

## 4.1 Introduction

Energy is one of the most significant driving forces for the growth of the nation. National Energy Balance 2017 published by Energy Commission Malaysia shows a significant increase in the consumption of energy from 1980 to 2017\. The increasing energy demand is due to the population and economic growth. Advances in technology have improved the quality of life and accelerate industrial revolution which leads to rapid growth in energy consumption [[1](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_001)]. [Figure 4.1](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_001) shows the electricity consumption by sectors in 2017\. Total electricity consumption of 146,524 GWh was recorded in 2017 [[1](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_001)]. This is a large amount of energy consumption. Based on the pie chart, industrial sectors had recorded the highest electricity consumption of 71,417 GWh which contributed 49% of the total energy consumption. Commercial sector consumed 30%, followed by 20% energy usage by residential and last 1% energy usage by agriculture sector and transportation sector. Both commercial and residential sectors combined to contribute half of the total electricity consumption. Hence, commercial and residential sectors have high potential to make curtailment of the tremendous total electricity consumption by consuming electricity efficiently and wisely.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-004_fig_001.png)

Figure 4.1: Electricity consumption by sectors in 2017 (adapted from National Energy Balance 2017).

Electricity consumers can play a huge role in achieving energy efficiency. However, the awareness of practicing energy efficiency does not show a strong presence in the society in Malaysia. Malaysia electricity generation fuel is indirectly subsidized. This is because national oil and gas company Petronas forgoes revenue to supply natural gas to TNB at below-market price. In fact, Malaysia’s electricity tariffs for the consumer, residential, industrial and commercial segments are among the lowest compared to other countries in the ASEAN region [[2](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_002)]. This is because the gas supply is subsidized by national gas company PETRONAS. Hence the electricity fee is not a burden and something worthy for most of the consumers to pay attention to. Electricity users do not consciously control their usage according to their needs. For example, many air-conditioning systems or lighting systems in a building are turned on all the time, even when there is no one in the building. As a result, a lot of energy has been wasted unnecessarily [[3](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_003)].

It is important to bring awareness to the consumers on their energy consumption. A detailed energy monitoring needs to be implemented to collect data in high volume, granularity and variability. However, the conventional metering system is not capable of providing the functionality needed to achieve this objective. The conventional meter requires utility company to send manpower reading the meter reading manually for billing, which is usually the end of the month. Users are unable to access the real-time consumption from the meter and consumer is unable to observe their energy usage pattern over time from the bill. Hence, they have no idea how to control their energy consumption effectively. Moreover, it cannot measure the disaggregated energy usage down to the level of individual devices which makes it hard for consumers to track down the device that consumes the highest energy. Compared to the conventional metering system, smart meters enabled with IoT collect detailed energy usage data from the consumers’ side on a real-time basis. They collect and transmit the energy usage information to the cloud system and later can be monitored and analyzed by consumers and utility company. The smart metering data provides insights to the utility company for strategic planning as well as informing the consumers about their energy consumption and billing in real time. Adoption of smart meter can enable various benefits such as real-time billing, demand response program, detection of energy fraud, blackouts warning. Therefore, limitation of the conventional meter could be overcome [[4](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_004)].

In Malaysia, the national electrical utility company, TNB, is moving toward smart metering to promote efficient usage of electricity. TNB began installing smart meters under a pilot project in Melaka and Putrajaya from 2014 to 2016 and plans to install smart meters 9.1 million households by 2026 all over the nation [[5](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_005)]. Promoting energy managementhelps the consumer to conserve energy and reduce the electricity billing. However, a low usage rate of the smart meterapplication was reported in its smart billing project. Statistics from systems’ logs show that only 10% of the users actively and regularly used the service [[6](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_006)]. Moreover, the services provided by the current TNB smart meter are still unable to provide consumers with detailed information to understand their energy consumption well and practice the energy saving effectively. The dashboard on the myTNB mobile application or portal webpage can show monthly and daily usage. The daily energy consumption in a day will be updated and reflected in the myTNB portal the next day [[7](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_007)]. However, users are unable to observe their energy usage in real time in an hourly or per-minute basis which reflects the actual trend of energy consumption.

Hence, a more efficient and effective energy monitoring solution can be developed to address the above limitations. This book chapter aims to present a comprehensive methodology to construct a cost-effective IoT enabled smart single-phase meter energy monitoring system. The design of the system is able to record per-minute energy information and display the data in the online via an IoT platform dashboard in real time. Users will be able to monitor the changes in electricity information within a short interval and control their devices accordingly. The system integration of an IEC standard meter, common industrial communication protocol (Modbus), Wi-Fi connectivity as well as IoT platform will be described in this book chapter. The hardware prototype proper casing design and installation as per the IEC industrial standard will be presented.

## 4.2 Literature review

### 4.2.1 Smart meter

In early days, the term “smart meter”referred to the device that can measure the electricity and remotely control the supply whenever necessary. It was called automated meter reading (AMR) which allows one-way communication and automated monthly reads, tampering detection and basic load profiling. Over time, the AMR required shorter interval data collection, on-demand reads and connecting into other commodities. The smart metering technologies further evolve and constitute to advanced metering infrastructure (AMI) which can facilitate two-way communication in the grid. The enhanced version of AMR has included the incorporation of service switching, time-based rates, remote programming, power quality measure and a dashboard-type user interface for real-time energy monitoring [3]. Today, a smart meter should have the following capabilities: real-time or near-real-time capture of electricity information; possibility of remote and local reading of the meter; remote load control of supply; possibility of integration with other commodity supplies.

Implementation of smart metering can bring various advantages to various stakeholders in the energy usage cycle and the environment. Mass deployment of smart metering in the smart grid environment allows the anticipated value to be maximized. The real-time energy usage can be monitored and the projected electricity billing can be viewed by energy consumers. Smart metering can measure the energy usage accurately which results in more accurate electricity billing, bringing great benefit to energy consumers. Time of use tariff can be introduced for the consumers to adjust their electricity consumption during off-peak hours to cut their bills. Consumers can enjoy a better power delivery services as power outages could be reduced [[8](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_008)].

For utility, the functionality of the smart meter could maximize the operational efficiency and effectiveness. With the massive smart metering data logging, load profiling and forecasting analysis can be carried out. Operators can gain full insights into the energy supply and demand which helps in planning and management of the whole power grid system [[9](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_009)]. Reserve margin of the power supply can be achieved which enables more efficient use of power resources and optimization of profit. The capability of the remote load controlling enables better management of billing and other consumer-related issues. Automated and remote meter reading eliminates the need for sending manpower to manually read the meter data. Real-time or near-real-time monitoring system improves reliability and efficiency. Hence, power outages can be reduced. Disturbance of power supply can be detected at the early stage before the circumstances become more critical and also faster maintenance can take place. By having a clear visualization of energy generation and utilization, dynamic pricing can be enabled according to the real-time information. Smart metering helps to capture the information in low- and medium-voltage network which is an important part in the development of smart grids.

The smart metering system helps to reduce environmental pollution. Fuel-fired power plants release a tremendous amount of greenhouse emission to the environment. The pollutants can cause global warming and lead to many other problems to biodiversity. The most vital contribution of smart metering is to reduce the usage of peaker plants that emit more emissions than normal power plants and prevent the need for new power plants by contributing to the proper distribution of existing power usage [[10](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_010)].

### 4.2.2 Smart energy monitoring system architecture proposed by other researchers

In recent years, energy management has become an important area among researchers for cost-saving and energy efficiency. [Figure 4.2](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_002) shows the number of publications on energy meter with IoT from 2014 to 2019 in the IEEE Xplore database. Based on the graph, it shows an uptrend in the research and development in the smart energy meter monitoring system using various IoT technologies, riding the wave of Industrial Revolution 4.0\. The energy monitoring system has been discussed for the past few decades and comprehensive researches and studies have been proposed. In the following subsections, the various system architecture will be reviewed, including power measurement unit, IoT connectivity technologies, central processing unit, features, data storage and user interface.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-004_fig_002.png)

Figure 4.2: Number of publications on energy meter with IoT.

### 4.2.3 Power measurement unit

Data capturing is the first process when it comes to energy monitoring system. There are various types and specifications of power measurement unit are available in the market to capture energy information. There are researchers who use hall effect current sensor to capture the current value [[11](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_011), [12](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_012)]. This type of sensor can detect direct current (DC) and alternating current (AC). They use the hall effect to convert current inputs into proportional analogue voltage outputs. They provide very low-cost solutions for AC current sensing and can be easily implemented with microcontrollers. However, the current sensor used in the references such as ACS712 module can only accept up to a maximum of 30 Ampere which is unable to handle the heavy electrical load.

Apart from the hall effect current sensor, some researchers used current transformer such as model SCT-013-000 in their system [[13](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_013), [14](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_014)]. Current transformers use electromagnetic induction to step down the alternating current to the acceptable input voltage for the microcontroller. It has the feature to measure very high currents and has low power consumption. Current transformers use noninvasive probing which is easier and safer. However, it is relatively large and costly compared to the current sensor for the same specification. For the voltage sensing unit, voltage transformer or potential transformer sensor such as ZMPT101B model was commonly deployed [[12](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_012), [14](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_014)]. It can measure DC or AC voltage and can be directly connected to the microcontroller input which is very convenient.

The devices stated above capture the information by converting electricity flowing in the circuit into readable signal for processor, while some other researchers used signal conditioning units to detect the indication signal produced by conventional meter. Signal conditioning sensors such as light-dependent resistor, optocoupler or optical sensor are used to detect the optical pulses generated by the LED attached on or the rotation of disc of the operating conventional meter [[15](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_015), [16](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_016), [17](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_017), [18](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_018)]. These sensors react to indication based on their characteristic and control the switching of the external power supply to send readable electrical signal to the microcontroller. However, these methods can only capture the power consumption data whereby the other important electricity information such as voltage, current, frequency and power factor are compromised. Moreover, the precision and accuracy of the data captured using the signal conditioning units may not be reliable.

### 4.2.4 Central processing unit

In smart energy monitoring system, the central processing unit interprets the electrical signal produced by the power measurement unit to get the correct value. There is a broad range of microcontrollers available in the market for different system specifications and considerations. Arduino boards are the common microcontroller in researchers [[19](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_019), [20](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_020), [21](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_021)], for example, Arduino UNO, Arduino Pro, Arduino Mega and Arduino Nano. This is because Arduino boards are provided with open forum support and on-board circuitry for sensor networks which are easier to implement. There are compatible official add-on shields that support extra features such as Wi-Fi connectivity and SD card developments. However, the normal models except for Arduino Nano occupy a lot of memory spaces for these extra features.

Besides, a microcontroller named Nucleo-64 board STM32 was proposed in another group of researchers [[22](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_022)]. The Nucleo board delivers high computational performance with low power consumption but it is bulky. There were some researchers who utilize the basic microcontroller integrated circuit such as ATMEGA328 instead of the development boards [[20](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_020), [23](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_023)]. It is cheaper and smaller in size, but it needs other supporting circuitry such as analogue to digital converter which could be more complicated in the implementation phase.

In recent years, NodeMCU ESP8266 has become popular among researchers. It is a development board integrated with wireless communication technologies which are suitable for projects looking for a compact solution since it is small and does not require extra modules for internet connectivity [[14](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_014), [18](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_018), [23](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_023), [24](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_024)]. ESP8266 chip runs on an 80 MHz system clock, 50kB of RAM and 4MB of flash memory. Its processing power and data storage are greater than the common Arduino boards. In terms of cost, they are cheaper than Arduino boards. However, NodeMCU board and ESP8266 module have lesser input and output pins compared to Arduino boards.

### 4.2.5 IoT connectivity technology

Current communication technologies can be categorized into wired-based and wireless-based. One of the researchers used Ethernet to allow the energy monitoring system to communicate with Wi-Fi router over a cable and transfer the data captured to the server [[21](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_021)]. Majority of previous researchers chose a wireless communication system to connect to the smart meter. Wi-Fi modules such as NodeMCU and ESP8266 Single on Chip (SoC) were chosen in the smart meter system [[15](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_015), [18](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_018)]. This module enables the energy monitoring system to establish Wi-Fi connection to the router or hotspot which subsequently connects to the internet. Other researchers installed the Arduino official Wi-Fi shield onto the Arduino board which is an easier implementation method [[11](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_011)].

Apart from Wi-Fi, ZigBee is a low-cost and low-power wireless technology based on IEEE 805.15.4 protocol. It is one of the promising wireless network technologies used by researcher [[22](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_022)]. GSM communication was adopted in some of the application to transmit important message upon specific events. It is a structural design used for mobile cellular communication in most of the countries. The GSM modem that possesses together with power supply and communication interfaces such as Sim900A-RS232 was implemented by the previous researchers [[17](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_017), [20](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_020)]. Other researchers used Arduino board to directly connect the board with the official GSM shield [[19](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_019)]. Besides that, Bluetooth communication technology has been adopted in the smart meter system. The metering system was connected to an Android application using a Bluetooth module HC-05\. Data would be updated regularly from the metering system to the device that runs the application for short-distance remote monitoring only [[13](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_013)]. However, the system still relies on Wi-Fi connectivity to send collected data to the cloud and store it.

### 4.2.6 Database and user interface

For real-time monitoring, cloud database is the most favorable solution to keep the massive amount of data. MySQL database was used in the smart energy monitoring researcher [[11](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_011), [13](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_013), [24](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_024)]. It is under an open-source license and compatible with many operating system and languages. It works well with large data set. However, a certain level of knowledge of the languages such as HTML and PHP is required to implement the database. There are researchers who build their own webpage to monitor the data. Web developing languages and tools such as HTML, JavaScript, AJAX, Bootstrap, CSS, PHP, and the Google Charts engine are used to design the webpage and display the data which has high flexibility [[11](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_011), [24](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_024)]. Researchers can customize the user interface according to the requirement, but it requires a certain extent of skill in language adopted. Users or visitors can access the information through the URL link once the host server is set up no matter it is local or remote.

In recent years, IoT platforms have become the preferred option by previous researchers in the smart energy monitoring system for IoT system [[25](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_025)]. The platforms such as ThingSpeak, Ubidots and FavorIoT offer free licenses for the researchers and educators. However, they have certain limitations in terms of update interval, data retention period and capacity. These platforms are good enough for small-scale research purposes. These platforms are designed to be very easy and quick to setup compared to the online database system. IoT platforms also have readily user interface (UI) design function and data visualization ability to create the user interface quickly and easily [[14](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_014), [15](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_015), [18](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_018)]. For example, user can insert the graph or widget to set up the dashboard that displays the information in graphical and numeric representation in a few steps. However, the dashboard design is not as flexible as using web developing languages because the options for visualization are limited to what provided by the platform. Projects that do not highly demand UI design could consider using the services offered by IoT platforms.

In addition, there are researchers who create the user interface on Android mobile application which allows the electricity usage data to be accessible by the consumer on their mobile device [[13](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_013), [14](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_014), [16](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_016)]. The application retrieves the data stored in the database or directly updates from the smart meter through the wireless connection. It is convenient and user-friendly for consumers since mobile devices have been immersed in people daily life. However, an IoT platform such as ThingSpeak has its complementary mobile application available for its users to access data on the cloud.

In summary, the important components of smart energy have been described in this section. Different energy meter monitoring implementation methods have their own advantages and disadvantages. Most of the papers do not elaborate in detail on the software implementation for real-time energy measurement and monitoring through an IoT platform. By adopting an industrial Modbus communication protocol and interface in the system design, existing energy meter using the same communication protocol standard can be implemented easily. Moreover, most of the previous researchers did not present a complete proof of concept design that is ready to use. Whereas this book chapter presents the insights on how to implement a user-friendly and cost-effective single-phase energy meter monitoring system through IoT platform.

## 4.3 Modbus protocol

Modbus protocol was published by Modicon Company in 1979 for use with its programmable logic controllers. Modicon is presently known as Schneider Electric. Modbus is a fundamental communication protocol which is widely applied in industries such as automation, industrial control, energy monitoring and management. It is an open communication protocol which is universal and easy to implement. It is located at layer seven of Open System Interconnection (OSI) model which works as an application layer messaging protocol. It allows client/server communication within all types of network architecture. It is currently implemented using TCP/IP over Ethernet, asynchronous serial transmission over a variety of media and other variants [[26](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_026)].

### 4.3.1 Modbus-RTU

Modbus protocol can be implemented over the serial line. The technical documentation “Modbus over serial line specification and implementation guide” published by Modbus Organization describes the implementation to facilitate interoperability between the devices using Modbus protocol. Modbus serial line protocol specifies a master–slave architecture which takes place at data link layer, level two of the OSI model. Modbus-RTU (remote terminal unit) is a commonly used Modbus serial protocol in industries as it is compulsory to be performed for all the Modbus devices. The term RTU refers to one of the serial transmission modes of the Modbus communication protocol as it is the default mode. Another transmission mode is known as ASCII (American Standard Code for Information Interchange) which is optional to be implemented. The main advantage of RTU mode compared to ASCII mode is its greater character density which provides better data throughput for the same baud rate. Different physical interfaces can be used to implement Modbus-RTU communication such as RS-485 and RS-232 [[27](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_027)].

Modbus protocol is a master–slave protocol which specifies only one master device and up to 247 slave devices are permitted in the same serial bus. Each of them should have a unique device address on the network so that they can be independently called by the master [[28](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_028)]. Only the master device can initiate the communication and make a request to the one or multiple slaves. Communication will not take place between slaves and they will not transmit a message without receiving the request sent by the master. Master device issues the request to the slaves in two modes which are unicast mode and broadcast mode. Unicast mode indicates the master issues message that addresses only an individual slave and the slave is the only one required to respond to the message after receiving and processing the request. Broadcast mode is used by the master node to send the message to every slave and do not expect a response. It is necessarily writing commands and has a reserved broadcast address.

### 4.3.2 RTU frame

Modbus-RTU makes use of a compact binary representation of data for serial line communication protocol. A Modbus message is constructed by the transmitting device into a frame with a known starting and ending point. This allows devices that receive a new frame to begin at the start of the message and to know when the message is completed. [Table 4.1](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_tab_001) shows the Modbus-RTUmessage frame starts with the 8 bits slave address and followed by the 8 bits function codes and data with a 16 bits cyclic redundancy-check (CRC) checksum as an error tests mechanism to ensure the reliability of data. Modbus-RTU is the most common implementation available for Modbus. [Figure 4.3](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_003) shows inter-frame delay where the RTU message frames are separated by idle periods. In RTU mode, message frames are separated by a silent interval of at least 3.5-character times. The entire message frame must be transmitted in a continuous stream. [Figure 4.4](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_004) shows the required silent interval between characters. The inter-character timeout should be less than or equal to 1.5-character times. If a silent interval between two characters takes place more than the timeout period of 1.5-character times, the message frame is considered incomplete and should be discarded by the receiver [[29](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_029)].

Table 4.1:Modbus RTU message frame.

| Silent interval | RTU message frame | Silent interval |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Start | Address | Function | Data | CRC check | End |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ≥3.5 char | 8 bits | 8 bits | N × 8 bits (up to 252 byte(s)) | 16 bits | ≥3.5 char |

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-004_fig_003.jpg)

Figure 4.3: Inter-frame delay (adapted from Modbus over serial line specification and implementation guide v1.02).

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-004_fig_004.jpg)

Figure 4.4: Inter-character time out (adapted from Modbus over serial line specification and implementation guide v1.02).

The information stored inside different fields of the RTUframe needs to be configured according to the specifications and requirements. The address is referring to the slave identifier (ID) which is known as unit number, unit address and slave address as well. The valid slave addresses are in the range of 0 to 247 in decimal. The individual slave devices are assigned addresses in the range of 1 to 247, where the address 0 is reserved for broadcast function. A master specifies the slave address in the address field of the message to make the request to the individual slave device. The request will be transmitted throughout network but only the respective slave node needs to respond. If the request is received by the slave device, the slave will return its response by placing its own ID in the response address field for acknowledgment. In broadcast mode, all the slaves are required to execute according to the request from the master when received but not necessary to reply the master node [[26](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_026)].

Function code indicates the action to be executed by the master. The function code field is coded in one byte. The valid codes are in the range of 1 to 255 in decimal. However, codes between 128 and 255 are reserved to be used for exception responses which indicate the type of error when occurs [[30](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_030)]. [Table 4.2](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_tab_002) shows the common Modbus function codes used. The actions are either read or write command and target to different types of Modbus data items which contain data for various purposes. There are a lot other function codes under this protocol.

Table 4.2:Modbus function codes.

| Function codes | Function |
| --- | --- |
| 0x01 | Read Coil |
| 0x02 | Read Discrete Input |
| 0x03 | Read Holding Register |
| 0x04 | Read Input Register |
| 0x05 | Write Single Coil |
| 0x06 | Write Single Register |

Different data items can be accessed using different function codes to execute various actions. These data items can be categorized into four different Modbus primary tables which have distinguishing characteristics. Coils and discrete input are single bit object while registers are 16-bit word item. Coils registers and holding register can be read or write; however, the discrete input and input register can be read only. Hence, the data stored discrete input and input register is provided by the I/O system [[26](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_026)]. Currently, each primary table can contain range of 0 to 65,535.

The next field in the Modbus message is the data field. The data are sent from the slave to master device containing additional information that the master device uses to take the action defined by the function code. For example, register addresses the quantity of items to be handled and the count of actual data bytes in the field. It may be not necessary in certain kinds of requests as the function code itself sufficient to explain the action [[31](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_031)].

The slave will echo normally when Modbus transaction is carried out successfully and the data field returns from the slave to master will contains the requested data. However, when there is an error found by the Modbus slave, it will return an exception code reply instead. The exception code is constructed by making the function code received set and occupying the function field of the message replied to the master. By referring to the exception code with the provided reference, error can be determined and troubleshot.

### 4.3.3 Modbus communication parameters

Implementation of communication of Modbus device on serial line using RTU transmission mode needs to be configured with the associated communication parameters. These parameters are baud rate and encoding format. Baud rate is the rate of data bit transferred per seconds. The baud rate recommended by the standard is 9,600 and 19,200 kbps. There are other available baud rates offered by different manufacturers for various specifications and the maximum speed can go up to 115,200 kbps. The encoding format refers to number of start bit, number of stop bit, parity bit, stored data type, word order, byte order and timeout period. Based on the Modbus-RTU standard, the 11-bit format for each byte is formed by 1 start bit, 8 data bits, 1 parity bit and 1 stop bit [[30](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_030)]. For parity bit, even parity is the default setting whereas other options may be used such as odd parity and no parity. However, one additional stop bit is required to occupy the character field when no parity bit is used [[32](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_032)]. For word order and byte order, big-Endian is commonly used to represent addresses and data which means the most significant byte sent first. The order inverses to big-Endian is called little-Endian.

### 4.3.4 RS-485 standard

RS-485 two-wire connection is the default electrical interface of Modbus-RTU; however, there are other options such as RS-232 and RS-485 four-wire connection. The initial RS-485 standard was approved in March 1998 to overcome the shortcomings of RS-232 and RS-422\. It was developed jointly by the Electronic Industries Association (EIA) and the Telecommunications Industry Association (TIA). RS-485 is officially known as EIA/TIA-485 where the EIA/TIA is usually replaced with the prefix RS (Recommended Standard) which is simpler and easier to use. There are various extensions of the RS-485 standard developed to fulfil a variety of applications such as Profibus and Fieldbus [[33](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_033)].

RS-485specifies a bidirectional and half-duplex data transmission standard which allows multiple drivers and receivers in the bus configuration. It is a point-to-point and multipoint system. The input sensitivity of receiver is ±200 mV which indicates a receiver can only read signals if the voltage level above +200 mV or below −200 mV. On the other hand, the driver output voltage is minimum ±1.5 V and maximum ±5 V. Based on the standard; the drive capacity is given 32 unit loads as each unit load is 12 kΩ. For instance, a maximum of 32 receivers with a minimum input impedance of 12 kΩ in parallel on the network without repeater There are many receivers designed with a higher input impedance to allow a higher number of unit loads to be connected on a bus without repeater, as long as the combined load looked at the driver side is within the limit [[34](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_034)].

The maximum driver load impedance is 54 Ω and AWG 24 twisted-pair cable is typically sufficient for Modbus implementation. In this case, the standard recommends 32 unit loads in parallel with two 120 Ω termination resistors with repeater [[32](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_032)]. A twisted pair refers a pair of wires of equal length and twisted together to reduce the radiated and received electromagnetic interference. In common practice, termination resistors with approximately the same value as the characteristic impedance of the wiring will be connected at the very end of the cable to mitigate reflections and increases the achievable data rate [[35](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_035)].

The differential signals paths of RS-485 standard is balanced because the signal on one wire is ideally the exact opposite of the signal on another wire. It provides sufficient margin for reliable data transmission even in the presence of noise and able to reject large common-mode voltages. Hence, RS-485 has widely used in various industries especially data acquisition and control applications as it allows longer transmission, higher data transmission speed and a larger drive capacity. It was adopted by many of the researches due to its robustness [[36](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_036), [37](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_037), [38](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_038)].

## 4.4 Methodology

### 4.4.1 Overview

This section proposes a comprehensive method to develop a smart energy meter monitoring system using IoT. The final product can monitor the energy information and allow user to observe the real-time data on the dashboard remotely. It uses an energy meter to obtain the energy information, and then the data stored in the register will be read by the microcontroller via serial communication protocol and lastly the system will update the information to the IoT platform using wireless connectivity technology. The prototype is designed with protective casing as per industry standard. The proposed system architecture includes hardware and software implementation. The main hardware devices consist of power measurement unit and the microcontroller with integrated Wi-Fi connectivity module which accounted for the important process such as data capturing, data processing and data sending. Other supporting components and accessories such as data converter module, power supply module and protective casing help in the overall system operating process while securing the reliability and safety purposes. Construction and installation procedures of the completed system are explained at the end of this section. Preliminary physical design and schematic diagram of the system are provided. Software implementation relates to the communication protocol, programming procedures and the setup of the IoT platform. The industrial serial communication protocol used will be introduced and implementation of the protocol to facilitate the data exchange between devices will be discussed. Tools and resources required such as programming software, library and IoT platform required will be explained. The final completed system is presented with the actual view of the product and the results collected.

### 4.4.2 Proposed system architecture

[Figure 4.5](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_005) shows the proposed system architecture. The incoming power from the socket outlet first passes through the energy meter and then goes through the circuit breaker and supply to the load. A small AC–DC power supply module steps down the incoming power to an acceptable range for the data converter module and microcontroller with Wi-Fi integrated. Electricity information is captured by the energy meter and the meter sends the data to the microcontroller via Modbus protocol. A data converter module is used to translate voltage level from the RS-485 interface into readable signal for the board. The microcontroller with Wi-Fi capability processes the data and sends it to the channel on the ThingSpeakIoT platform using the internet. The data collected on the ThingSpeak channel is stored into the cloud database and visualized on an online dashboard where users can always view their energy profile at remote area.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-004_fig_005.jpg)

Figure 4.5: Proposed system architecture.

## 4.5 Hardware design

### 4.5.1 Power measurement unit

DDM18SD energy meter is chosen as the power measurement unit of the system. It is a DIN rail single-phase digital energy meter. It can directly and accurately measure the load active energy consumption in the single-phase AC networks of rated frequency 50 or 60 Hz. It can capture reading of current, voltage, active power, frequency and power factor from the load. Operating voltage range accepts 230/240 V[AC] single-phase power under Malaysian electricity standard while the current specification is 100/5 A which could handle normal electrical and electronics appliances and machinery. Its technical performances completely conform to international standards IEC 62053-21 for class 1 single-phase active energy meters. Besides, it comes with RS-485 interface and supporting Modbus-RTU communication protocol for remote reading function.

### 4.5.2 Microcontroller with integrated Wi-Fi connectivity module

WeMos D1 Mini R2 is used to read the data register of the energy meter and send the information to the IoT platform after processing. It is a miniature wireless microcontroller development board based on IEEE 802.11 (Wi-Fi) protocol. It combines with ESP8266 wireless microcontroller into a full-fledged development board. It features 4MB of flash memory, 80 MHz of system clock, around 50KB of usable RAM and an on-chip Wi-Fi transceiver. Programming the D1 mini is as simple as programming any other Arduino based microcontroller as the module includes a built-in micro-USB interface allowing the module to be programmed directly from the Arduino IDE. It requires the ESP8266 board package to be added via board manager with no additional hardware. On-board 3.3 V voltage regulator module allows the devices to be powered directly from a USB port via micro-USB.

### 4.5.3 Modbus RS-485 to TTL converter module

A RS-485 to TTL converter module is required for the WeMosboard to read data from RS-485 interface correctly. RS-485 uses differential signal that is immune to interference and noise, and is suitable for long-distance transmission, while TTL is designed for logic level and is used for board-level communication. This data converter module is based on Maxim MAX485 interface chip which converts the voltage level from RS-485 port to respective TTL standard, and vice versa. This module operates at 5 V supply voltage and has low power consumption.

### 4.5.4 Power supply module

An AC–DC power supply module is used to step down the input voltage240 V[AC] from the power socket to 5 V[DC] to power up the WeMos D1 Mini and TTL-RS-485 converter. According to the specifications, it allows input voltage range from 90 to 264 V[AC] and regulates the output voltage to 5 V[DC]. It has a low power consumption of 3 W and ultra-small dimension which could fit into the narrow space of DIN rail enclosure. It also features high efficiency, low ripple and noise as well as overload and short circuit protection.

### 4.5.5 Protection devices

Schneider Electric C60N circuit breaker was chosen and installed to protect circuits against short-circuit currents and overload currents. It provides protection of persons against indirect contact with neutral system. It follows the technical specifications are according to IEC 60947-2.

DIN rail enclosureencapsulates the internal electrical and electronic components which include the WeMos D1 Mini, TTL-RS-485 converter, AC–DC power supply module. This form of enclosure is ideally used for limited space. It allows printed circuit boards and other standard electrical components to be safely installed and protected, mitigating the chance of damage and deterioration. An enclosure designed for a DIN rail can easily be mounted to the rail via a screw or hook, providing quick installation and disassembly.

A 6-way distribution box (DB) is chosen to enclose the whole electrical system. Circuit breaker, energy meter and the DIN rail enclosure are to be installed in the distribution box. A DB usually contains the main electrical supply system for any commercial or residential entity. The incoming power cable comes into the distribution board and then distributed in the secondary circuits via circuit breaker. It is important for optimal performance of devices with safety. DBs come with busbars, DIN rail and neutral link for installation and wiring routing purposes.

Meter board comes in addition to hold the entire system in place. All the electrical and electronic devices and components enclosed in the DB and the other accessories are fixed on the meter board. Male 3-pin power inlet socket is secured on the side of the board to plug in the power cord which can be connected to the supply. Socket outlet is installed on the meter board to connect with the load. Wiring and terminals are covered behind the meter board where the amendments can be carried out directly from the back when necessary.

Socket outlet is used for the load to be plugged in. The socket used is complying with electrical equipment standard of MS 589-2, SS 145-2 and BS 1363-2\. The outlet poles configuration is two poles and one earth, and its rated current is 13 A at 230 V AC 50 Hz. Thicker cables are needed to deal with the larger flowing current. Cables with a cross-sectional area of 4 mm² are used to connect from the incoming power supply to the electrical devices such as circuit breaker, energy meter and power socket. The wiring color code follows old UK standard, which is red for the live cable, black for the neutral line, and green for the earth.

### 4.5.6 Prototype construction

The physical design which consists of electrical components and parts were designed to be installed to the meter board as shown in [Figure 4.6](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_006). Interconnection and wiring are completed according to the schematic diagram shown in [Figure 4.7](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_007). The physical design is performed using AutoCAD web application. It allows user to edit, create and view CAD drawings in the web browser on any computer which is very convenient. Interconnection diagram is designed using online EasyEDA Designer. Software installation is not required and it allows user to create the drawing in the browser directly. It is a very powerful electronics design software that has various open-source projects and extensive component libraries for user to create their schematic diagram and PCB layout.

The physical design of the prototype takes into consideration to assemble the parts and components in one unit. Small electrical electronics components are encapsulated in the DIN rail enclosure which is then installed on the DIN rail of the distribution box together with the energy meter and the circuit breaker. The 3-pin plug socket is located at the side of the meter board where the power cord will be connected to the power supply. Socket outlet on the meter board is designated for any electrical load measurement. It is located below the DB box because it allows the wiring routing. A handle will be installed on the top of the meter board which allows user to carry around and hang on somewhere when it is in use.

Wiring routing and interconnection of the system are illustrated in [Figure 4.7](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_007). First, the incoming power passes through the energy meter. Then it flows to the circuit breaker and lastly to the socket outlet on the meter board. The electrical load can be connected to the supply by plugging on the socket. The power supply also goes to the AC–DC power supply module to step down the voltage for the RS-485-TTL converter module and the WeMos board. Port A and B of the RS-485 interface at the bottom of the energy meter are connected to the respective terminals at the converter module. The output pins of the converter module are connected to the respective pin of the WeMos board.

The prototype is designed to compact and easy to use. The criteria of the design include safety by incorporating the protective device as well as proper electrical isolation. Reliability of the device performance is taken into consideration by using enclosure and cover that prevent dust, moisture and deterioration.

## 4.6 Software development

### 4.6.1 Measurement unit Modbus-RTU register

Modbus RS-485 communication protocol is adopted for remote meter reading function of DDM18SD energy meter. The information of Modbus protocol is extracted from the user manual. It is very important to understand the respective parameters and configuration since every device may have a little difference from each other. Based on the manual, the encoding formatused is complying with the Modbus-RTU standard and requires an even parity bit. The 11-bit format of each byte comprises one stop bit, eight data bits, one parity bit and one stop bit. The baud rate supported are 1,200, 2,400, 4,800, or default 9,600 bps. Besides, the protocol support even parity in default and are configurable into odd and none. The slave address is 1 by default and can be configured manually within the range specified by the Modbus standard. There are two types of register used in the meter which is data register and parameter register according to the manual. The data register is read-only using 0 × 04 Modbus command while later can be read and written, using Modbus command 0 × 03 and 0 × 10, respectively. The type of action can be executed on the data register and parameters register indicate their Modbus register types which are input register and holding register respectively. The communication parameters are stored in the parameter register which can be configured using the function code 0 × 06 to overwrite the value stored in the respective holding registers. Data register addresses from voltage to frequency are accessed in this research since the main focus is to monitor the energy information instead of controlling or manipulating the device. The documentation of Modbus register addresses is not standardized; hence, it is important to note that the addresses might be provided in the different standard. The register addresses provided in the manual is written in base-0 which means it is the exact register address required to be accessed. However, manufacturers may provide the register reference numbers instead of register addresses which have an offset value of 1\. The data read from the meter conforms to the IEEE-754 standard which is according to 32-bit 4-byte single-precision floating-point format. The data registers information is tabulated in [Table 4.3](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_tab_003).

Table 4.3:Data register list.

| Data register address | Register description |
| --- | --- |
| High byte | Low byte | Parameter | Unit | Format | Mode |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 00 | 00 | Voltage | V | Float | Read-only |
| 00 | 08 | Current | A | Float | Read-only |
| 00 | 12 | Active power | W | Float | Read-only |
| 00 | 36 | Frequency | Hz | Float | Read-only |

### 4.6.2 Validating measurement unit Modbus registers reading

ModbusExplorer application in MATLAB can be used to test the remote meter reading function of energy meter and get a better understanding of how the Modbus-RTU protocol works. It offers a user interface to easily set up reads and writes easily. It is also able to show a live plot to view the values and manage the reads for multiple data addresses easily. [Figure 4.8](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_008) shows the connection setup testing between the energy meters to the laptop. A RS-485 to USB converter module is used to enable the laptop to read data from energy meter via RS-485 interface. The converter module is based on the chip CH320 and can automatically identify and control the data transmission direction without any handshake signals.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-004_fig_008.jpg)

Figure 4.8: Connection setup of testing between the energy meter and the laptop.

By using the Modbus Explorer, the configuration of the Modbus communication parameters and the meter reading function can be carried out easily. First, a new connection to the device is configured. The connection can be Modbus serial or Modbus TCP/IP. Modbus serial is selected since it implements Modbus-RTU protocol. Next, the parameters of the Modbus protocol adopted by the energy meter such as parity, baud rate, number of stop bits, number of data bits, timeout period, byte order, word order and server id are filled in as shown as in [Figure 4.9](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_009). The register address and register type are assigned, and the application will try to read data from that register address before proceeding to confirm the parameter settings. The configuration process can proceed if there is a valid value read from the register.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-004_fig_009.jpg)

Figure 4.9: Configuration of Modbus communication specification.

Finally, the information of the data registers to be read is inserted and the results can be retrieved and shown in the table. From the interface, values of different parameters can be read in real-time as shown as in [Figure 4.10](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_010). The results obtained are as expected. The voltagereading recorded is 243.1 V which is around 240 V and the frequency is exactly 50 Hz which comply with the Malaysia electricity standard. The current reading is 0.206 A, power factor is 0.458 and active power is 22.3 W which are considered acceptable since it was connecting to a laptop. Hence, by referring to the settings that can read the value successfully and correctly, the Modbus transaction process can be coded in the system program accordingly.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-004_fig_010.jpg)

Figure 4.10: Live register data reading from ModbusExplorer application.

[Figure 4.10](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_010) shows that single precision is used for the data format and values are read correctly. The single-precision represents IEEE-754 single-precision floating-point standard which used as the data format of the energy meter according to the manual. A single-precision floating-point number can represent a very wide range of value (approximately ± 10^(38.53)) with an accuracy of approximately 7 decimal digits. IEEE-754 single-precision states the structure of the binary representation of the floating-point data in 32-bit data. However, each register can only store 16 bits and therefore 32-bit data need to be separated and stored in two consecutive registers. The Modbus protocol does not clearly define how should the data be stored and transmitted in the registers, so the manufacturers can have their own design for the organization of data structure. Hence, it is important to read the data from the register according to the design implemented by the manufacturers. Data register reading must be executed in the correct byte order and word order. The word order indicates the sequence of the two words of data that stored in consecutive registers which can be big-Endian (the high-order word is stored first, followed by the low-order word) or vice versa, which called as little-Endian. The same concept applies to byte order. There are 2 bytes of data in a word so either higher byte is stored and transmitted first, followed by the lower byte or vice versa. The order is also named as big-Endian and little-endian respectively. After reading the data in the correct order, the high word and low word need to be combined to obtain a complete 32-bit binary data structure.

The combined 32-bit data needs to be interpreted in IEEE-754 format instead of a normal common binary value. According to the IEEE-754 standard, the most significant bit is the sign bit (S), with 0 for positive numbers and 1 for negative numbers. The following 8 bits represent exponent (E). It is a biased exponent where a bias is added to the actual exponent to get the stored exponent. Both positive and negative exponents are represented. The remaining 23 bits represent mantissa (M). It is a normalized mantissa. The mantissa is part of a number in scientific notation or a floating-point number, consisting of its significant digits. A normalized mantissa is one with only one 1 to the left of the decimal. It is complicated to compute the actual value from each component of the IEEE-754 data structure using the calculation method in coding. Floating-point is a common variable type recognized by computer system hence conversion can be achieved easily using the built-in function. Typecasting function can be used to cast the value into the desired value type. In the system program, the combined 32-bit data is stored as unsigned 32-bit integer. Therefore, it needs to be cast to the floating-point data type so that the system interprets the data correctly.

### 4.6.3 System program flow

[Figure 4.11](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_011) shows the program flow of the real-time energy metermonitoring system. First, initialization of the Modbus communication protocol, Wi-Fi connectivity, software serial pin, NTP server and ThingSpeak channel are carried out. All the required parameters are configured to ensure the processes in the loop are run properly. Next, WeMos Wi-Fi board will try to connect with the home router until the internet connection is established. After that, the currenttimestamp is checked from the Network Time Protocol (NTP) server. NTP is an internet protocol used to synchronize the clocks of computers to within a few milliseconds of universal coordinated time (UTC).It consistently monitors the current time and wait until the next minute which completes time interval of a minute basis before reading data from the data register of the energy meter. Data reading from the data registers of the energy meter is carried out using Modbus-RTU communication protocol in every minute. Data received by the WeMos board will be processed according to IEEE-754 single precision floating binary form to organize it in the correct format and obtain the respective value. Lastly, the processed data are sent to the ThingSpeak channel using post method. It is a request method supported by HTTP that is designed to send data packets to a server from a specified resource. Whenever the Wi-Fi connection is disconnected, the program will be started to reconnect before running the loop.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-004_fig_011.jpg)

Figure 4.11: Program flow of the real-time energy meter monitoring system.

### 4.6.4 Programming platform

In this project, Arduino Integrated Development Environment (IDE) is used to write the program according to the concept illustrated above. It is an open-source platform that where the comprehensive documentation and extensive resource are available online. It is widely used by students, developer and researchers because of its user-friendly interface and comes with community support. It allows code writing to be easy and has high compatibility with most of the board available in the market. It runs on Windows, Mac OS X, and Linux. This software supports both C and C++ languages.

### 4.6.5 Board core and libraries used

Board cores are necessary to make new microcontrollers compatible with the Arduino IDE and the existing sketches and libraries. Board Manager inside the Arduino IDE provides a list of developed cores for Arduino boards. ESP8266 microcontroller used by WeMos D1 Mini board is not an Arduino board hence a third part core needs to be added. ESP8266 Arduino Core is used for this project. It brings support for the ESP8266 chip to the Arduino environment, allowing user to write sketches, using familiar Arduino functions and libraries, and run them directly on ESP8266 without an external microcontroller. To install a third-party platform package, additional URL link needs to be added in the Preferences window of Arduino IDE before adding the core using the Board Manager. The board package URL link used is [https://arduino.esp8266.com/stable/package_esp8266com_index.jsonandtheversion](https://arduino.esp8266.com/stable/package_esp8266com_index.jsonandtheversion) of the board package to be installed in the board manager is v2.7.2\. It is important to use the compactible version because some of the older versions contains bugs and unable to perform well.

Libraries are useful tools that can save a lot of works as it contains groups of codes and functions that are created by the developers for certain purposes. There are a few libraries used to build the codes according to the program flow [[39](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_ref_039)]. The board package comes with the ESP8266 libraries allows ESP8266 board to perform the certain required functions. For example, ESPSoftwareSerial library and ESP8266Wi-Fi library. WeMos D1 Mini R2 has only 1 serial port which is the micro-USB port and the pair of transmitter and receiver pins. Only one of the pins can be used as serial port at a time. The micro-USB port always used to print the result through the Serial Monitor of Arduino IDE. Hence, an extra serial port is needed to receive signal from the energy meter. ESP software serial library is used in the codes. It allows users to convert two digital pins into a software serial port. This library codes add the encoder and decoder on the two digital pins to allow them to use for transmitting and receiving signal just like the serial port. It is mainly for ESP8266 boards. Pins D1 and D2 in the ESP8266 board are used as the software serial port in this project. Another library namely ESP8266WiFi from the package is used to provide ESP8266-specific Wi-Fi methods to connect to network easily. The other required libraries are ModbusMaster, NTPClient and Wi-FiUDP which can be downloaded from the library manager. ModbusMaster library makes the programming of Modbus communication easy, enables communication with Modbus slaves over RS232/485 (via RTU protocol). NTPClient library codes enable an NTP client to connect to a time server, get the time and keep it in sync. Wi-FiUdp library is associated with NTPClient library to facilitates request to NTP server using User Datagram Protocol (UDP) like opening a UDP port, sending and receiving UDP packets, and so on. [Table 4.4](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_tab_004) shows the key functions utilized from these libraries to develop the system program. The functions listed in the table are those important codes used in this project. There are many other functions provided by these libraries which can be utilized for other actions or purposes depending on the researchers or users.

Table 4.4:Key functions utilized from the libraries in system program.

| Library | Key function | Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ESPSoftwareSerial | begin() | Initiate serial configuration such as baud rate, data, parity, and stop bits |
| ESP8266WiFi | begin() | Initializes the Wi-Fi library’s network settings such as SSID and password |
| status() | Check Wi-Fi connection status |
| connect() | Allow client to connect to the IP address and port specified in the constructor |
| print() | Print data to the server that a client is connected to |
| ModbusMaster | begin() | Assigns the Modbus slave ID and serial port. |
| readInputRegisters() | Execute Function 0 × 04 to read data from Input Register |
| readHoldingRegisters() | Execute Function 0 × 03 to read data from Holding Register |
| Ku8MBSuccess() | Check whether the Modbus transaction is successful |
| getResponseBuffer() | Retrieve data from response buffer |
| NTPClient&WiFiUDP | NTPClient() | Specific NTP server and UTC time offset |
| begin()  | Initialize the NTP client |
| update() | Get the current date and time from the NTP server |
| getSeconds() | Returns current second data |

### 4.6.6 IoT platform

The cloud database and real-time dashboard of the smart energy meter monitoring system are implemented using services provided by ThingSpeak. It is an IoT analytics platform service that allows user to collect, visualize and analyze real-time data streams in the cloud. It makes IoT system prototyping easy as it simplifies the configuration and coding of the database and user interface design at the backend. It integrates with MATLAB to provide powerful data analysis function. Action on the data such as alert message and control can be carried out using third-party services. ThingSpeak provides free license for the students, researchers and educators. The data storage offered under free license is sufficient for small-scale project. Data can be permanently stored on the cloud unless the user deletes it. Message update interval limit is 15 s which fulfil for this project since the system requires to update data to the cloud on a minute basis. Besides that, there is large community support where the users can exchange information conveniently. Paid licenses are available in ThingSpeak for more features.

### 4.6.7 Setup of ThingSpeak

In ThingSpeak application, channels are defined to store data. Under the free license, each channel has eight fields that can hold any type of data, besides three fields for location data and one for status data. Once data is collected in a channel, it can be analyzed and visualized using ThingSpeak. Key settings are channel name, description and field. First, a unique name is created for the ThingSpeak channel and a brief description is inserted. For this project, the channel setting is set up as shown in [Figure 4.12](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_012). Name and description are filled up accordingly and four out of eight fields are being utilized for the parameters monitored.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-004_fig_012.jpg)

Figure 4.12: ThingSpeak channel settings.

ThingSpeak dashboard can display data in a graphical and numerical representation. Visualization charts and widgets such as gauge and numerical display are provided as shown in [Figure 4.13](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_013). Voltage data collected from the energy meterover an hour are visualized in graphical method in [Figure 4.13(a)](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_013) while [Figure 4.13(b)](#9783110713794_010746.xhtml_b_9783110714043-004_fig_013) shows the last updated voltage value using the gauge widget. User can easily observe the electricity information remotely in real-time.

![](graphic/converted/b_9783110714043-004_fig_013.jpg)
